Each iteration: render every fixture → inspect failures against the expectations file → extend SKILL.md and/or <code>html-patterns.md</code> and/or <code>html-skeleton.md</code> to cover the failing pattern → re-render. Repeat until every pattern fixture and both control fixtures pass.<br>\n<strong>Done when:</strong> Every pattern fixture and both control fixtures pass on two consecutive runs (a light-touch variance check appropriate to eyeball evaluation). SKILL.md still inside the 180–240 line target.<br>\n<strong>Dependencies:</strong> Task 4</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"ta[REDACTED_SK]\">Task 6 — Description-improver pass</h3>\n<p><strong>Description:</strong> Run skill-creator's description-improver to tune the SKILL.md <code>description:</code> frontmatter for triggering accuracy on trigger phrases from the approved plan (\"render this doc as HTML\", \"make this reviewable\", \"convert design doc to HTML\", \"I want to share this plan\").<br>\n<strong>Done when:</strong> Description-improver reports an improved or equal triggering score vs. baseline; the tuned description is committed to SKILL.md.<br>\n<strong>Dependencies:</strong> Task 5</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"ta[REDACTED_SK]\">Task 7 — Wire up routing in README and using-this-pack</h3>\n<p><strong>Description:</strong> Add a <code>render-html</code> row to the engineering/review table in <code>README.md</code>; add a routing entry to <code>skills/using-this-pack/SKILL.md</code> mapping the trigger phrases above to <code>render-html</code>.<br>\n<strong>Done when:</strong> <code>README.md</code> lists render-html with its one-line description; <code>using-this-pack/SKILL.md</code> routes the trigger phrases to <code>render-html</code>.<br>\n<strong>Dependencies:</strong> Task 6</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"ta[REDACTED_SK]\">Task 8 — End-to-end verification on a real artefact</h3>\n<p><strong>Description:</strong> Run <code>/render-html</code> on a real artefact already in the repo (an existing <code>docs/designs/*.md</code> or <code>docs/tasks/*.md</code>, not a synthetic fixture). Validate against the full verification checklist from the approved plan in two browsers.<br>\n<strong>Done when:</strong> All checks pass: (a) output written alongside source, (b) opens in Chrome AND Safari via <code>file://</code> with zero network requests in DevTools Network tab, (c) no console errors/warnings, (d) every H2/H3 reachable via TOC, (e) every applicable pattern renders correctly, (f) file size &lt; 500KB, (g) print preview hides the TOC and breaks pages reasonably, (h) re-rendering the same source produces a diff only in provenance/timestamp lines.<br>\n<strong>Dependencies:</strong> Task 7</p>\n\n<h2 id=\"resolved-decisions\">Resolved decisions</h2>\n\n<ul>\n  <li><strong>Fixtures location:</strong> <code>skills/render-html/evals/</code> (co-located with the skill). Safe because the Claude Code plugin loader only auto-loads <code>SKILL.md</code> from each skill directory — subdirectory contents are read on demand by the agent, not eagerly loaded into context. Confirmed against the loader behaviour observed in <code>.claude-plugin/plugin.json</code> discovery. Re-check if skill-creator publishes contrary best-practice guidance.</li>\n  <li><strong>Pattern detection:</strong> hybrid — heading-name heuristics are the primary signal, with an optional explicit override via a fenced annotation (<code> ```pattern:alternatives </code>) for cases where authors want precision or where their headings don't match the catalogue. Rationale: pde skills already produce consistent heading names (design-doc enforces \"Problem\", \"Alternatives considered\", etc. by anatomy), so heading-name detection works out of the box for the most common inputs. The annotation escape hatch preserves the \"any structured md\" property without forcing every author to mark up their docs. Pure-heuristic loses on renames; pure-annotation breaks zero-effort rendering; hybrid avoids both failure modes.</li>\n  <li><strong>Output file collision:</strong> overwrite with a confirmation prompt. When <code>&lt;source&gt;.html</code> exists, the skill must prompt before writing. Affects Task 1's acceptance criterion (below).</li>\n  <li><strong>Eval harness scope for v0.1:</strong> define fixtures + expected-output descriptions and <em>eyeball</em> results during iteration. Defer automated scoring to v0.2. Context: skill-creator's full loop wants (a) fixture inputs, (b) machine-checkable expectations, (c) running claude-with-the-skill against each fixture, (d) automated pass/fail scoring, (e) iterate until scores stabilise. Automated scoring catches regressions when the pattern catalogue grows, but for the initial 8 patterns the iteration cost of eyeballing is lower than the cost of building the scoring harness. Revisit once we add a 9th pattern or a regression appears.</li>\n</ul>\n\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
}

**Walking skeleton (Task 1, where we are):** prose body + TOC + dark/light auto. Deliberately boring. Goal is "any md → openable HTML with zero network requests" — the plumbing.

**Pattern catalogue (Tasks 4–5):** the Thariq-style treatments. Side-by-side alternatives columns, kanban Now/Next/Later, dependency-arrow timelines for task lists, callout blocks for Problem/Context/Decision, ICE score chips, annotated diffs. That's where this doc would actually get visually interesting (the task list becomes a timeline with arrows; the resolved-decisions section becomes callout blocks).

Two options:

1. **Stay with the plan** — extract the shell to a reference (Task 2), build fixtures (Task 3), then iterate the pattern catalogue (Tasks 4–5). The boring skeleton gives us a stable base before adding visual logic.

2. **Jump ahead** — skip Task 2/3 and start adding patterns directly so you can see the payoff faster. Tradeoff: harder to know what regressed when, no fixtures to check against.

My recommendation is (1) — the staging exists because pattern logic on an unstable shell is hard to debug — but if you want to see the Thariq-style output sooner, (2) is fine and we can backfill fixtures later. Which?

1	---
2	name: portfolio-themes
3	description: >
4	  Seven-category taxonomy for classifying roadmap items by strategic purpose.
5	  Source: Shreyas Doshi. Used in roadmap-shape and capacity allocation under Rule B3.
6	type: reference
7	cited_by:
8	  - skills/roadmap-shape/SKILL.md
9	---
10	
11	# Portfolio Themes
12	
13	Portfolio themes are Shreyas Doshi's taxonomy for classifying roadmap items by their strategic purpose. They are not a prioritisation method — they do not produce a rank order. Their purpose is to name what kind of work an item represents so the team can allocate capacity deliberately across the portfolio rather than letting it drift toward whichever category has the loudest advocates.
14	
15	Rule B3 mandates that every roadmap item is classified into one of the seven themes, and that capacity allocation across themes is made explicit each planning cycle. A roadmap that does not do this will drift — typically toward customer specials and incrementals, at the expense of tech foundation and differentiators.
16	
17	## The seven themes
18	
19	| Theme | What it is | The implicit risk if over-indexed |
20	|---|---|---|
21	| **Differentiators** | Capabilities competitors don't have that customers care about | High investment without validation becomes expensive waste |
22	| **Table-stakes** | Capabilities needed just to be taken seriously in the market | Under-investment means customers don't evaluate you; over-investment means parity at high cost |
23	| **Incrementals** | Improvements to things you already do | Easy to over-invest; improvement signals are noisy and satisfaction rarely jumps |
24	| **Embarrassments** | Parts of the product the team is quietly ashamed of; broken windows | Ignored, they compound. Addressed, they pay trust dividends internally and externally. |
25	| **Customer specials** | Features driven by specific accounts or sales relationships | A roadmap that becomes 50%+ customer specials is a sales roadmap; product complexity grows non-linearly |
26	| **Tech foundation** | Platform, infrastructure, technical debt reduction | Under-investment causes velocity collapse over time; over-investment delays customer value |
27	| **Speculative bets** | High-risk, high-upside exploration; options on the future | Easy to starve; also easy to let absorb capacity without accountability |
28	
29	## Using themes at roadmap shape
30

  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/pde-skills/references/html-skeleton.md",
  "content": "---\nname: html-skeleton\ndescription: >\n  Self-contained HTML shell used by the render-html skill. Defines the single-file\n  document template: inline CSS (system font, sticky TOC sidebar, prose column,\n  dark-mode auto, print stylesheet), inline scrollspy JS, and the hard self-containment\n  rule.\ntype: reference\ncited_by:\n  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md\n---\n\n# HTML skeleton\n\nThe render-html skill wraps every markdown body in the shell below. The shell is the **only** mechanism by which output styling and navigation arrive — no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. That rule is load-bearing for the skill's \"opens via `file://` with no network\" guarantee; reviewers attach these files to emails and store them in private wikis where CDN URLs silently break.\n\n## The hard rule\n\nAnything injected into the rendered output must satisfy all three:\n\n- **No `<link rel=\"stylesheet\">`.** All CSS lives in the inline `<style>` block.\n- **No `<script src=...>`.** All JS lives in the inline `<script>` block at the end of `<body>`.\n- **No remote `<img>` URLs.** Inline SVG is preferred; base64 data URIs are allowed when an SVG is impractical. Remote URLs (`http:`, `https:`, protocol-relative `//`) are not.\n\nIf a future pattern needs richer interactivity (charts, diagrams, syntax highlighting), inline the minimal library or hand-write the SVG. Adding a CDN is not an option.\n\n## Substitution tokens\n\nThe skill substitutes these into the shell at render time. Treat them as the contract between the skill's workflow and this reference.\n\n| Token | Source | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `{{title}}` | The source markdown's first H1 | Goes in both `<title>` and the page `<h1>`. |\n| `{{source-path}}` | Path of the input `.md` file, relative to the repo root if possible | Shown in the provenance meta line. |\n| `{{render-date}}` | ISO date (YYYY-MM-DD) of the render | Provenance only — do not include time, so re-renders on the same day produce no diff. |\n| `{{toc-entries}}` | One `<li>` per H2/H3 from the source, with class `h2` or `h3` and an `href` to the heading's slug | Slugs are kebab-case from the heading text, deduplicated with `-2`, `-3` if needed. H1 is the title, not a TOC entry. |\n| `{{body-html}}` | The markdown body converted to HTML, with H2/H3 carrying `id=\"<slug>\"` matching the TOC links | Pattern-specific treatments (later iterations) substitute richer markup here. |\n\n## The shell\n\n```html\n<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<title>{{title}}</title>\n<style>\n  :root { --fg: #111; --muted: #666; --bg: #fff; --rule: #e5e5e5; --accent: #0a58ca; }\n  @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {\n    :root { --fg: #eee; --muted: #999; --bg: #111; --rule: #2a2a2a; --accent: #6ea8fe; }\n  }\n  html, body { margin: 0; padding: 0; background: var(--bg); color: var(--fg); }\n  body { font: 16px/1.55 ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, \"Segoe UI\", Roboto, sans-serif; }\n  .layout { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 260px 1fr; max-width: 1100px; margin: 0 auto; }\n  nav.toc { position: sticky; top: 0; align-self: start; max-height: 100vh; overflow-y: auto; padding: 24px 16px; border-right: 1px solid var(--rule); font-size: 14px; }\n  nav.toc h2 { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); margin: 0 0 8px; }\n  nav.toc ul { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 0; }\n  nav.toc li { margin: 4px 0; }\n  nav.toc li.h3 { padding-left: 12px; font-size: 13px; }\n  nav.toc a { color: var(--fg); text-decoration: none; }\n  nav.toc a:hover, nav.toc a.active { color: var(--accent); }\n  main { padding: 32px 40px; max-width: 72ch; }\n  main h1 { font-size: 28px; margin: 0 0 4px; }\n  main .meta { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13px; margin-bottom: 32px; }\n  main h2 { font-size: 22px; margin-top: 40px; padding-top: 8px; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  main h3 { font-size: 17px; margin-top: 24px; }\n  main pre { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.08); padding: 12px 14px; border-radius: 6px; overflow-x: auto; font-size: 13px; }\n  main code { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 0.92em; }\n  main table { border-collapse: collapse; margin: 12px 0; }\n  main th, main td { border: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 6px 10px; text-align: left; }\n  footer { grid-column: 1 / -1; padding: 24px 40px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 12px; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  @media print {\n    nav.toc { display: none; }\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n  }\n  @media (max-width: 720px) {\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n    nav.toc { position: static; max-height: none; border-right: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div class=\"layout\">\n<nav class=\"toc\" aria-label=\"Table of contents\">\n<h2>Contents</h2>\n<ul>\n  {{toc-entries}}\n</ul>\n</nav>\n<main>\n<h1>{{title}}</h1>\n<p class=\"meta\">Rendered from <code>{{source-path}}</code> on {{render-date}}</p>\n{{body-html}}\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  // Scrollspy: highlight the TOC entry for the section currently in view.\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n```\n\n## Design notes\n\n- **System font stack** — `ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, \"Segoe UI\", Roboto, sans-serif`. Zero network, native to every platform, sensible fallbacks. Web fonts would be a CDN dependency.\n- **Prose width cap** — `max-width: 72ch` on `<main>`. Long-line prose is fatigue-inducing; reviewers skim faster at typographic widths.\n- **Sticky TOC** — `position: sticky; top: 0; max-height: 100vh; overflow-y: auto;`. Stays visible while scrolling, scrolls internally for very long TOCs.\n- **Scrollspy** — IntersectionObserver-based, ~12 lines. Highlights the TOC entry for whichever section is in view. The `rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px'` biases the active region to the upper third of the viewport, so the active entry matches where the reader's eye actually is.\n- **Dark mode** — `prefers-color-scheme: dark` media query swaps CSS custom properties. Honours OS setting; no toggle UI (out of scope for v0.1).\n- **Print** — TOC hidden, grid collapses to single column. Reviewers who print to PDF for offline annotation get something usable.\n- **Responsive** — below 720px the TOC moves above the body. The skill is review-oriented (desktop-first) but mobile shouldn't be broken.\n\n## When pattern treatments arrive\n\nPattern-specific markup (side-by-side alternatives, kanban Now/Next/Later, callouts, dependency timelines) substitutes into `{{body-html}}` — the shell itself does not change. Pattern CSS extends the inline `<style>` block via additions, not replacements, so the prose path remains the default fallback for any markdown the skill does not classify.\n"
}

}\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n  }\n  @media (max-width: 720px) {\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n    nav.toc { position: static; max-height: none; border-right: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div class=\"layout\">\n<nav class=\"toc\" aria-label=\"Table of contents\">\n<h2>Contents</h2>\n<ul>\n  {{toc-entries: <li class=\"h2\"><a href=\"#slug\">Heading</a></li> ...}}\n</ul>\n</nav>\n<main>\n<h1>{{title}}</h1>\n<p class=\"meta\">Rendered from <code>{{source-path}}</code> on {{render-date}}</p>\n{{body-html}}\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  // Scrollspy: highlight the TOC entry for the section currently in view.\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n```",
  "new_string": "## Artefact template\n\nThe HTML shell — `<!doctype html>` template, inline CSS, scrollspy JS, and the substitution-token contract (`{{title}}`, `{{source-path}}`, `{{render-date}}`, `{{toc-entries}}`, `{{body-html}}`) — lives in `references/html-skeleton.md`. Read that file in Step 5 and substitute the tokens. The shell is not duplicated here so a single edit to the skeleton updates both this skill and any future pattern-aware extensions of it."
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/pde-skills/references/html-patterns.md",
  "content": "---\nname: html-patterns\ndescription: >\n  Pattern catalogue for the render-html skill. Each entry maps a recognisable\n  markdown shape to a richer HTML treatment — side-by-side columns, kanban,\n  timelines, callouts, score chips. The skill consults this file during\n  conversion (Step 4) and substitutes the matching markup into {{body-html}}.\ntype: reference\ncited_by:\n  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md\n---\n\n# HTML pattern catalogue\n\nThe render-html skill walks the markdown body section by section. For each section, it checks the patterns below in order. The **first match wins** — if no pattern matches, the section renders as plain prose (the walking-skeleton fallback).\n\nEvery pattern is defined by:\n\n1. **Detect** — the markdown signal that triggers the treatment. Cheap to check; conservative (false-negative is fine, false-positive is bad — wrongly-treated content is harder to debug than untreated).\n2. **HTML** — the output markup. All class names are prefixed `p-` so they don't collide with prose styles.\n3. **CSS** — additions appended to the shell's inline `<style>` block. The shell's prose styles remain the fallback; pattern CSS only adds, never overrides.\n\nThe hard self-containment rule from `html-skeleton.md` applies to every pattern: no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. Inline SVG is preferred for visual elements.\n\n---\n\n## P1 — Alternatives considered → side-by-side columns\n\n**Detect.** A section whose heading text matches `/alternatives?( considered)?/i`, OR a section containing two-to-four sibling H3s each followed by a bulleted list whose first item starts with `Pros:` or `Cons:` (case-insensitive). Maximum four columns — beyond that, fall back to prose (columns become unreadable).\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<section class=\"p-alternatives\">\n  <div class=\"p-alt-col\">\n    <h3>Alternative A — short name</h3>\n    <dl>\n      <dt>Pros</dt><dd><ul><li>...</li></ul></dd>\n      <dt>Cons</dt><dd><ul><li>...</li></ul></dd>\n      <dt>Cost</dt><dd>...</dd>\n      <dt>Reversibility</dt><dd>...</dd>\n    </dl>\n  </div>\n  <!-- repeat per alternative -->\n</section>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-alternatives { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(220px, 1fr)); gap: 16px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-alt-col { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 14px 16px; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.03); }\n.p-alt-col h3 { margin-top: 0; font-size: 15px; }\n.p-alt-col dl { margin: 0; }\n.p-alt-col dt { font-size: 11px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 10px; }\n.p-alt-col dd { margin: 4px 0 0; font-size: 14px; }\n.p-alt-col ul { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Each column is a `<section>` with a unique `<h3>`; the columns still appear in DOM order, so screen readers read them sequentially. No keyboard interaction needed — pure layout.\n\n---\n\n## P2 — Task list with dependencies → vertical timeline\n\n**Detect.** A section containing an ordered list (or sequence of H3s named `Task N — title` / `Step N`) where two or more entries mention \"depends on\", \"after\", \"blocks\", or \"blocked by\" in their first paragraph. Pure task lists without dependency language stay as ordinary ordered lists.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ol class=\"p-timeline\">\n  <li class=\"p-task\" id=\"task-1\">\n    <div class=\"p-task-marker\" aria-hidden=\"true\">1</div>\n    <div class=\"p-task-body\">\n      <h3>Task 1 — name</h3>\n      <p class=\"p-task-deps\">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>\n      <p>Task description prose…</p>\n    </div>\n  </li>\n  <!-- repeat per task; dependency lines link to #task-N -->\n</ol>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 16px 0; position: relative; }\n.p-timeline::before { content: \"\"; position: absolute; left: 14px; top: 0; bottom: 0; width: 2px; background: var(--rule); }\n.p-task { position: relative; padding-left: 44px; margin-bottom: 18px; }\n.p-task-marker { position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; width: 30px; height: 30px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--bg); border: 2px solid var(--accent); color: var(--accent); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; font-weight: 600; font-size: 13px; }\n.p-task-body h3 { margin: 4px 0 6px; font-size: 16px; }\n.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13px; margin: 0 0 8px; }\n.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href=\"#task-2\">Task 2</a>`); marker is `aria-hidden`.\n\n---\n\n## P3 — Design-doc blocks → coloured callouts\n\n**Detect.** H2 headings whose text matches one of: `Problem`, `Context`, `Constraints`, `Decision` (or `Recommended approach`), `Consequences`, `Open questions`. Apply per matching H2 — sibling non-matching H2s render as prose.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-problem\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\"><span class=\"p-callout-icon\" aria-hidden=\"true\">▶</span> Problem</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\">\n    <!-- prose -->\n  </div>\n</section>\n```\n\nMapping (used for `p-callout-<key>`): `problem` → red, `context` → grey, `constraints` → amber, `decision` → blue, `consequences` → green, `open-questions` → purple.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-callout { margin: 20px 0; border-left: 4px solid var(--cb, var(--rule)); border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; background: var(--cbg, rgba(127,127,127,0.04)); padding: 12px 16px; }\n.p-callout-head { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--cb, var(--muted)); margin-bottom: 6px; font-weight: 600; }\n.p-callout-icon { display: inline-block; margin-right: 4px; }\n.p-callout-body > :first-child { margin-top: 0; }\n.p-callout-body > :last-child { margin-bottom: 0; }\n.p-callout-problem      { --cb: #dc3545; --cbg: rgba(220,53,69,0.06); }\n.p-callout-context      { --cb: #6c757d; --cbg: rgba(108,117,125,0.06); }\n.p-callout-constraints  { --cb: #fd7e14; --cbg: rgba(253,126,20,0.07); }\n.p-callout-decision     { --cb: #0a58ca; --cbg: rgba(10,88,202,0.06); }\n.p-callout-consequences { --cb: #198754; --cbg: rgba(25,135,84,0.06); }\n.p-callout-open-questions { --cb: #6f42c1; --cbg: rgba(111,66,193,0.06); }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the heading text carries the meaning. Border + label work for users who can't perceive the hue.\n\n---\n\n## P4 — NFRs table → highlighted numeric table\n\n**Detect.** Markdown table inside a section whose heading matches `/non-functional|NFRs?/i`, OR any table whose header row contains one of: `Target`, `SLO`, `p95`, `p99`, `Latency`, `Throughput`, `Availability`, `Budget`.\n\n**HTML.** Standard `<table>` with `class=\"p-nfr\"`; cells that parse as numbers (optionally with units `ms`, `s`, `%`, `/s`, `KB`, `MB`) get `class=\"p-num\"`.\n\n```html\n<table class=\"p-nfr\">\n  <thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>Target</th><th>Current</th></tr></thead>\n  <tbody>\n    <tr><td>p95 latency</td><td class=\"p-num\">200 ms</td><td class=\"p-num\">340 ms</td></tr>\n  </tbody>\n</table>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-nfr { width: 100%; }\n.p-nfr th { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.06); font-size: 13px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.03em; color: var(--muted); }\n.p-nfr td.p-num { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; }\n.p-nfr tbody tr:hover { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.04); }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Standard table semantics; `<thead>`/`<tbody>` preserved.\n\n---\n\n## P5 — Open questions → checklist with owner / due\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?/i`. Body is a bullet list; each bullet may end with parenthetical metadata `(owner: name, due: 2026-06-01)` or markdown task-list syntax `- [ ]` / `- [x]`.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ul class=\"p-questions\">\n  <li class=\"p-q\">\n    <input type=\"checkbox\" disabled> <!-- checked if [x] -->\n    <span class=\"p-q-text\">Should we use Postgres or DynamoDB for the audit log?</span>\n    <span class=\"p-q-meta\">@anton · due 2026-06-01</span>\n  </li>\n</ul>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-questions { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-q { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto; gap: 10px; align-items: baseline; padding: 8px 0; border-bottom: 1px dashed var(--rule); }\n.p-q input[type=checkbox] { transform: translateY(2px); }\n.p-q-text { color: var(--fg); }\n.p-q-meta { color: var(--muted); font-size: 12px; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** The checkbox is `disabled` (read-only display); the parenthetical metadata is preserved as visible text, not buried in `title=` attributes.\n\n---\n\n## P6 — Diff / before-after → two-column annotated diff\n\n**Detect.** Two adjacent fenced code blocks within the same H3 / paragraph where the first is labelled (in info string or preceding line) `before`, `was`, `old`, `current`, and the second `after`, `now`, `new`, `proposed`. Also: any code block with info string `diff` renders inline with `+`/`-` line colouring.\n\n**HTML (two-column).**\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-diff\">\n  <div class=\"p-diff-side p-diff-before\">\n    <header>Before</header>\n    <pre><code>…</code></pre>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-diff-side p-diff-after\">\n    <header>After</header>\n    <pre><code>…</code></pre>\n  </div>\n</div>\n```\n\n**HTML (inline diff).** Standard `<pre><code class=\"language-diff\">` with each line wrapped: `<span class=\"p-diff-add\">+ …</span>`, `<span class=\"p-diff-del\">- …</span>`.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-diff { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 12px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-diff-side header { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 4px; }\n.p-diff-before pre { border-left: 3px solid #dc3545; }\n.p-diff-after  pre { border-left: 3px solid #198754; }\n.p-diff-add { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.12); display: block; }\n.p-diff-del { background: rgba(220,53,69,0.12); display: block; }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-diff { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Headers (`Before` / `After`) carry the meaning; colour stripe is supplemental.\n\n---\n\n## P7 — Roadmap Now / Next / Later → three-column kanban\n\n**Detect.** A section whose body contains three sibling H2s or H3s with text exactly matching (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant): `Now`, `Next`, `Later`. If only two of the three are present, still render as a kanban with the missing column shown empty.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-kanban\">\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"now\">\n    <header>Now</header>\n    <ul>\n      <li>Item one</li>\n      <li>Item two</li>\n    </ul>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"next\"> … </div>\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"later\"> … </div>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-kanban { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 12px; margin: 20px 0; }\n.p-kanban-col { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; padding: 12px 14px; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.03); min-height: 120px; }\n.p-kanban-col header { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); padding-bottom: 8px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); margin-bottom: 10px; font-weight: 600; }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"now\"]   header { color: #198754; }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"next\"]  header { color: #0a58ca; }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"later\"] header { color: #6c757d; }\n.p-kanban-col ul { padding-left: 18px; margin: 0; font-size: 14px; }\n.p-kanban-col li { margin: 6px 0; }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-kanban { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Each column is keyboard-navigable as ordinary text; the header colour is supplemental to the label.\n\n---\n\n## P8 — ICE scores → coloured score chips\n\n**Detect.** Markdown table whose header row contains `Impact`, `Confidence`, `Ease` (in any order, plus an optional `ICE` or `Score` column), with numeric cells `1`–`10`.\n\n**HTML.** Numeric cells become chips coloured by value (red 1–3, amber 4–6, green 7–10).\n\n```html\n<table class=\"p-ice\">\n  <thead><tr><th>Idea</th><th>Impact</th><th>Confidence</th><th>Ease</th><th>ICE</th></tr></thead>\n  <tbody>\n    <tr>\n      <td>Onboarding revamp</td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"green\">9</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">5</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">3</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">135</span></td>\n    </tr>\n  </tbody>\n</table>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-ice .p-chip { display: inline-block; min-width: 28px; padding: 2px 8px; border-radius: 999px; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 12px; text-align: center; font-weight: 600; }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"red\"]   { background: rgba(220,53,69,0.18); color: #dc3545; }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"amber\"] { background: rgba(253,126,20,0.18); color: #fd7e14; }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"green\"] { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.18); color: #198754; }\n.p-ice td:not(:first-child) { text-align: center; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the numeric value is the source of truth. Chips render as plain numbers in print and to screen readers.\n\n---\n\n## Fallback\n\nA section that matches no pattern renders with the shell's standard prose styles (`<h2>`, `<h3>`, `<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, `<pre>`). Fallback is the default, not the exception — false-positive pattern matches degrade review trust faster than missing treatments.\n\n## Adding a new pattern\n\nWhen a new pattern earns its slot:\n\n1. Add a section here following the same six-part structure: heading, **Detect**, **HTML**, **CSS**, **Accessibility**, brief rationale.\n2. Number it sequentially (`P9 — …`) so cross-references stay stable.\n3. Confirm the CSS only adds new selectors prefixed `p-` — it must not override any rule in `html-skeleton.md`.\n4. If the detection signal could overlap an existing pattern, document the precedence rule explicitly. The first-match-wins ordering of this file is the resolution mechanism.\n"
}
```

For v0.1 do not apply pattern-specific treatments — every section renders as straightforward prose. Pattern detection lands in a later iteration.",
  "new_string": "**Step 4 — Convert body**\nWalk the markdown section by section (H2-delimited). For each section, read `references/html-patterns.md` and check the patterns in declared order — first match wins. If a pattern matches, emit its HTML template and append its CSS rules to the collected pattern-CSS buffer. If nothing matches, render the section as straightforward prose using standard CommonMark semantics (H2/H3 with anchor IDs from Step 3, paragraphs, lists, blockquotes, fenced code blocks preserving language hints, inline code, tables, links). Prose is the fallback, not the exception — false-positive pattern matches degrade reviewer trust faster than missing treatments."
}
```

1	# Tasks: render-html
2	
3	Design doc: /Users/anton/.claude/plans/expressive-knitting-quiche.md (approved plan; serves as the design doc for this skill)
4	Implementation tool: `/skill-creator:skill-creator` (used for drafting, evals, iteration, and description tuning)
5	Last updated: 2026-05-17
6	
7	## How skill-creator changes the shape
8	
9	skill-creator's loop is: draft → test prompts → run skill against prompts → quantitative + qualitative eval → rewrite → repeat → finally run the description-improver. That replaces the sequential "author one pattern at a time" structure with an iterate-on-evals loop. Each HTML pattern becomes a test case in the eval suite, not a discrete authoring task. Pattern coverage is then measured rather than asserted.
10	
11	## Task list
12	
13	### Task 1 — Walking skeleton via skill-creator draft
14	**Description:** Invoke `/skill-creator:skill-creator` to draft `skills/render-html/SKILL.md` and `.claude/commands/render-html.md` covering anatomy compliance (per `docs/skill-anatomy.md`) and a minimal inline HTML shell that converts any md file to a self-contained `<source>.html` with TOC of H2/H3 + prose body.
15	**Done when:** Running `/render-html <some-existing-md-in-this-repo>` writes a sibling `.html` that (a) opens via `file://` in Chrome with no console errors and zero network requests, (b) contains a TOC listing every H2/H3 from the source, (c) renders every md heading and paragraph, (d) if the target `.html` already exists, the skill prompts for overwrite confirmation before writing. SKILL.md passes a manual anatomy check (all required sections present in order, `[GATE]`s preserved, frontmatter validates).
16	**Dependencies:** none
17	
18	### Task 2 — Extract HTML shell into reference file
19	**Description:** Move the inline `<!doctype html>` shell out of SKILL.md into `references/html-skeleton.md` (system font stack, ~70ch prose cap, sticky TOC sidebar, scrollspy JS ≤ 50 lines, prefers-color-scheme dark mode, print stylesheet). Document the hard rule: no external `<link>`/`<script src>`/remote images. SKILL.md cites the reference.
20	**Done when:** Re-rendering the Task 1 fixture produces an HTML byte-identical to before (modulo provenance/timestamp lines); SKILL.md line count drops accordingly and stays inside the 180–240 target.
21	**Dependencies:** Task 1
22	
23	### Task 3 — Build the fixture + expectations set for the iterate loop
24	**Description:** Create one synthetic md fixture per HTML pattern from the approved plan (alternatives-considered, ta[REDACTED_SK], design-doc-blocks, NFRs-table, open-questions, diff/before-after, roadmap-now-next-later, ICE-scores) plus two control fixtures: an unstructured bulleted brainstorm (must degrade to clean prose) and an omnibus fixture combining every pattern. For each fixture, write a plaintext expectations file describing what the rendered HTML must contain (specific structures, no console errors, size < 500KB). v0.1 evaluation is by eyeball — run the skill against each fixture, open the output in a browser, check against the expectations file. No automated scoring harness yet (see Resolved decisions).
25	**Done when:** Fixtures live under `skills/render-html/evals/fixtures/`; each has a sibling expectations file; the Task 1 walking-skeleton draft has been eyeballed against every fixture and the baseline (most pattern fixtures fail, prose + control fixtures pass) recorded in a notes file.
26	**Dependencies:** Task 1
27	
28	### Task 4 — Scaffold the pattern catalogue
29	**Description:** Create `references/html-patterns.md` with an introduction and one empty section per pattern (matching the fixtures from Task 3). Each section template: *when to apply*, *source md shape*, *HTML/CSS treatment*, *accessibility notes*. SKILL.md cites this reference in its workflow step 3.
30	**Done when:** `references/html-patterns.md` exists with 8 named sections; SKILL.md references it.
31	**Dependencies:** Task 2, Task 3
32	
33	### Task 5 — Iterate via skill-creator until all pattern fixtures pass
34	**Description:** Run the skill-creator iterate loop with eyeball evaluation. Each iteration: render every fixture → inspect failures against the expectations file → extend SKILL.md and/or `html-patterns.md` and/or `html-skeleton.md` to cover the failing pattern → re-render. Repeat until every pattern fixture and both control fixtures pass.
35	**Done when:** Every pattern fixture and both control fixtures pass on two consecutive runs (a light-touch variance check appropriate to eyeball evaluation). SKILL.md still inside the 180–240 line target.
36	**Dependencies:** Task 4
37	
38	### Task 6 — Description-improver pass
39	**Description:** Run skill-creator's description-improver to tune the SKILL.md `description:` frontmatter for triggering accuracy on trigger phrases from the approved plan ("render this doc as HTML", "make this reviewable", "convert design doc to HTML", "I want to share this plan").
40	**Done when:** Description-improver reports an improved or equal triggering score vs. baseline; the tuned description is committed to SKILL.md.
41	**Dependencies:** Task 5
42	
43	### Task 7 — Wire up routing in README and using-this-pack
44	**Description:** Add a `render-html` row to the engineering/review table in `README.md`; add a routing entry to `skills/using-this-pack/SKILL.md` mapping the trigger phrases above to `render-html`.
45	**Done when:** `README.md` lists render-html with its one-line description; `using-this-pack/SKILL.md` routes the trigger phrases to `render-html`.
46	**Dependencies:** Task 6
47	
48	### Task 8 — End-to-end verification on a real artefact
49	**Description:** Run `/render-html` on a real artefact already in the repo (an existing `docs/designs/*.md` or `docs/tasks/*.md`, not a synthetic fixture). Validate against the full verification checklist from the approved plan in two browsers.
50	**Done when:** All checks pass: (a) output written alongside source, (b) opens in Chrome AND Safari via `file://` with zero network requests in DevTools Network tab, (c) no console errors/warnings, (d) every H2/H3 reachable via TOC, (e) every applicable pattern renders correctly, (f) file size < 500KB, (g) print preview hides the TOC and breaks pages reasonably, (h) re-rendering the same source produces a diff only in provenance/timestamp lines.
51	**Dependencies:** Task 7
52	
53	## Resolved decisions
54	
55	- **Fixtures location:** `skills/render-html/evals/` (co-located with the skill). Safe because the Claude Code plugin loader only auto-loads `SKILL.md` from each skill directory — subdirectory contents are read on demand by the agent, not eagerly loaded into context. Confirmed against the loader behaviour observed in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` discovery. Re-check if skill-creator publishes contrary best-practice guidance.
56	- **Pattern detection:** hybrid — heading-name heuristics are the primary signal, with an optional explicit override via a fenced annotation (` ```pattern:alternatives `) for cases where authors want precision or where their headings don't match the catalogue. Rationale: pde skills already produce consistent heading names (design-doc enforces "Problem", "Alternatives considered", etc. by anatomy), so heading-name detection works out of the box for the most common inputs. The annotation escape hatch preserves the "any structured md" property without forcing every author to mark up their docs. Pure-heuristic loses on renames; pure-annotation breaks zero-effort rendering; hybrid avoids both failure modes.
57	- **Output file collision:** overwrite with a confirmation prompt. When `<source>.html` exists, the skill must prompt before writing. Affects Task 1's acceptance criterion (below).
58	- **Eval harness scope for v0.1:** define fixtures + expected-output descriptions and *eyeball* results during iteration. Defer automated scoring to v0.2. Context: skill-creator's full loop wants (a) fixture inputs, (b) machine-checkable expectations, (c) running claude-with-the-skill against each fixture, (d) automated pass/fail scoring, (e) iterate until scores stabilise. Automated scoring catches regressions when the pattern catalogue grows, but for the initial 8 patterns the iteration cost of eyeballing is lower than the cost of building the scoring harness. Revisit once we add a 9th pattern or a regression appears.
59	
```

Validate against the full verification checklist from the approved plan in two browsers.</p>\n      <p class=\"p-task-field\"><span class=\"p-task-label\">Done when</span>All checks pass: (a) output written alongside source, (b) opens in Chrome AND Safari via <code>file://</code> with zero network requests in DevTools Network tab, (c) no console errors/warnings, (d) every H2/H3 reachable via TOC, (e) every applicable pattern renders correctly, (f) file size &lt; 500KB, (g) print preview hides the TOC and breaks pages reasonably, (h) re-rendering the same source produces a diff only in provenance/timestamp lines.</p>\n      <p class=\"p-task-deps\">Depends on: <a href=\"#task-7\">Task 7</a></p>\n    </div>\n  </li>\n\n</ol>\n\n<h2 id=\"resolved-decisions\">Resolved decisions</h2>\n<ul>\n  <li><strong>Fixtures location:</strong> <code>skills/render-html/evals/</code> (co-located with the skill). Safe because the Claude Code plugin loader only auto-loads <code>SKILL.md</code> from each skill directory — subdirectory contents are read on demand by the agent, not eagerly loaded into context. Confirmed against the loader behaviour observed in <code>.claude-plugin/plugin.json</code> discovery. Re-check if skill-creator publishes contrary best-practice guidance.</li>\n  <li><strong>Pattern detection:</strong> hybrid — heading-name heuristics are the primary signal, with an optional explicit override via a fenced annotation (<code>```pattern:alternatives</code>) for cases where authors want precision or where their headings don't match the catalogue. Rationale: pde skills already produce consistent heading names (design-doc enforces \"Problem\", \"Alternatives considered\", etc. by anatomy), so heading-name detection works out of the box for the most common inputs. The annotation escape hatch preserves the \"any structured md\" property without forcing every author to mark up their docs. Pure-heuristic loses on renames; pure-annotation breaks zero-effort rendering; hybrid avoids both failure modes.</li>\n  <li><strong>Output file collision:</strong> overwrite with a confirmation prompt. When <code>&lt;source&gt;.html</code> exists, the skill must prompt before writing. Affects Task 1's acceptance criterion (below).</li>\n  <li><strong>Eval harness scope for v0.1:</strong> define fixtures + expected-output descriptions and <em>eyeball</em> results during iteration. Defer automated scoring to v0.2. Context: skill-creator's full loop wants (a) fixture inputs, (b) machine-checkable expectations, (c) running claude-with-the-skill against each fixture, (d) automated pass/fail scoring, (e) iterate until scores stabilise. Automated scoring catches regressions when the pattern catalogue grows, but for the initial 8 patterns the iteration cost of eyeballing is lower than the cost of building the scoring harness. Revisit once we add a 9th pattern or a regression appears.</li>\n</ul>\n\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
}

1	# Roadmap
2	
3	Last updated: 2026-05-03
4	Cycle: Now / Next / Later (no fixed date horizon — solo personal project)
5	
6	---
7	
8	## Strategic context
9	
10	Phase 1 MVP is largely complete: Funda scraping, bike time + commute enrichment, deal-breaker filtering, weighted scoring, listing feed + detail, shortlist, scoring profile config, admin dashboard.
11	
12	**This cycle's priorities (inferred from PRD phases — confirm before committing):**
13	1. Close the partner-participation gap: the primary user's partner cannot participate in listing evaluation without a shared view. This is the single biggest functional hole relative to the PRD vision.
14	2. Close the "works while you sleep" promise: timed digest email is validated for a build slot; push notifications need a gap audit before committing.
15	3. Validate neighbourhood intelligence as the next differentiation layer before building it.
16	
17	> ⚠️ **Input gap**: strategic priorities are inferred from PRD §2–§6 rather than an explicit cycle goal statement. If priorities have shifted (e.g. active search is paused, or a specific Phase 3 item is time-sensitive), adjust slot assignments before acting on this roadmap.
18	
19	---
20	
21	## Capacity allocation
22	
23	Solo developer. Approximate allocation for the current cycle:
24	
25	| Category | % of capacity |
26	|---|---|
27	| Differentiators (`diff`) | 35% |
28	| Incrementals (`incr`) | 15% |
29	| Tech foundation (`tech`) | 5% |
30	| Speculative bets (`spec`) | 0% |
31	| Embarrassments (`emb`) | 0% |
32	| Customer specials (`cs`) | 0% |
33	| Validation | 30% |
34	| KTLO | 15% |
35	| **Total** | **100%** |
36	
37	---
38	
39	## Theme distribution
40	
41	| Item | Theme | ICE | Confidence | Kano | Slot |
42	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
43	| Shareable shortlist (partner reactions + notes) | `diff` | 160 | 5 | Must-be | **Now** |
44	| Daily digest email (Melbourne morning) | `incr` | 210 | 5 | Performance/Excitement | **Next** |
45	| Map view + cycling routes | `incr` | — | — | Performance | Later |
46	| Pararius / HousingAnywhere adapters | `diff` | — | — | Performance | Later |
47	| Score overrides after viewings | `incr` | — | — | Performance | Later |
48	| Proximity scoring (parks, transit) | `incr` | — | — | Performance | Later |
49	| Push notifications (high-score listings) | `incr` | 84 | 2 | Performance | **Validation** |
50	| Neighbourhood RAG ("About this area") | `diff` | 42 | 2 | Excitement | **Validation** |
51	| Q&A agent (shortlist chat) | `spec` | 12 | 1 | Excitement | **Validation** |
52	
53	---
54	
55	## Now
56	
57	### Shareable shortlist — partner reactions and notes
58	
59	**Problem:** For a two-person household evaluating Amsterdam rentals remotely, we believe the absence of a shared view into the scored shortlist is causing joint listing decisions to be made via screenshots and messaging apps rather than within the tool, fragmenting the evaluation workflow and preventing the partner from participating with structured reactions or notes.
60	
61	**Success criterion:** Partner accesses the shared shortlist URL and leaves at least one reaction or note on a listing within 72 hours of the first share event.
62	
63	**Appetite:** 2 weeks. Cap, not estimate. If full scope (reactions + notes + combined status) doesn't fit, cut to: share URL → partner read-only view → like/dislike reaction. Notes and combined status move to Next.
64	
65	**Theme:** `diff` — no Dutch rental platform supports partner co-review without accounts.
66	
67	**ICE:** 8 × 5 × 4 = 160
68	
69	**Kano:** Must-be. Without it, the partner cannot participate. Absence blocks the core household workflow.
70	
71	---
72	
73	## Next
74	
75	### Daily digest email — Melbourne morning timing
76	
77	**Problem:** For an expat user in the Melbourne timezone checking Amsterdam listings, we believe the absence of a proactive daily summary delivered at the right local time is causing the app to fail its "works while you sleep" promise: new listings require active app-checking rather than arriving in the user's inbox when they are ready to act.
78	
79	**Theme:** `incr`
80	
81	**ICE:** 6 × 5 × 7 = 210
82	
83	**Note:** Sequence after push notification validation resolves. If push notifications are validated and built, design digest and push together to avoid notification fatigue (a listing shouldn't appear in both a push alert and the same morning's digest). If push is killed, digest ships standalone.
84	
85	---
86	
87	## Later
88	
89	These are strategic hypotheses, not commitments. No scoping until they move to Next.
90	
91	### Map view with listing locations and cycling routes
92	Helps users orient listings spatially. No confirmed gap in current tool — users may not need it if scores are sufficient.
93	
94	### Pararius / HousingAnywhere adapters
95	Expands coverage beyond Funda. Priority rises if Funda coverage proves insufficient or listings become scarce.
96	
97	### Score overrides after viewings
98	Post-viewing corrections to provisional scores. Low priority until listings are being visited in person.
99	
100	### Proximity scoring (parks, supermarkets, transit via Places API)
101	Enriches the Neighbourhood Vibe section with data. Complements — and possibly supersedes — the Neighbourhood RAG validation bet.
102	
103	---
104	
105	## Validation slots
106	
107	### Push notifications — high-scoring listings
108	
109	**Hypothesis:** Proactive alerts for high-scoring listings would reduce the window between ingestion and user awareness, reducing the risk of missing a listing before acting on it.
110	
111	**Why it's here:** Manual checking habit confirmed in PRD. But the platform already has a near-real-time ingestion path via forwarded email alerts. The user-awareness gap (listing ingested → user notified) may not exist or may already be closed. Problem is plausible but not confirmed.
112	
113	**Validation method:** Gap audit (≤2 hours, no code).
114	1. Review last 2–4 weeks of ingested listings. For each high-scoring listing: when ingested vs. when first viewed. Is there a consistent multi-hour delay?
115	2. Trace the Funda email alert → forwarded → ingestion flow. Does ingestion already produce a visible user-facing signal?
116	3. If gap is confirmed: assess whether a triggered mailer (easy, ~1 day) closes it before committing to web push infrastructure.
117	
118	**Confidence target:** Raise from 2 → 6. If gap audit shows no meaningful delay: kill or defer. If confirmed: move to idea bank and schedule after digest email ships.
119	
120	**Current ICE:** 7 × 2 × 6 = 84. If validated: projected 7 × 6 × 6 = 252.
121	
122	---
123	
124	### Neighbourhood RAG — "About this area" panel
125	
126	**Hypothesis:** An AI-generated neighbourhood brief (free text + concerns + citations) would replace the user's manual Reddit/Google research step per listing, accelerating the shortlist-to-viewing-decision workflow.
127	
128	**Why it's here:** Problem confirmed (manual step exists). Solution unvalidated — Dutch-language source coverage, RAG quality for hyper-local facts, and hallucination risk are all unknown.
129	
130	**Validation method:** 1-day spike.
131	1. Pick 3–5 Amsterdam neighbourhoods (one well-reviewed, one contested, one obscure).
132	2. Fetch top Reddit/Google results manually for each.
133	3. Prompt Claude: generate free-text brief + concerns list + citations from that content.
134	4. Evaluate: Is the output accurate? Does it surface real concerns? Would you trust it over doing the research yourself?
135	
136	**Confidence target:** Raise from 2 → 6. If spike output is not trustworthy: kill or reshape as curated-links fallback (lower impact, no hallucination risk).
137	
138	**Current ICE:** 7 × 2 × 3 = 42. If validated: projected 7 × 7 × 3 = 147.
139	
140	---
141	
142	### Q&A agent — shortlist chat
143	
144	**Hypothesis:** A natural-language chat interface on the shortlist would let users compare listings and ask cross-listing questions faster than reading individual cards.
145	
146	**Why it's here:** Problem unconfirmed. The tool already answers "which listings are worth my time" via scoring and ranking. Whether residual unanswered questions exist after seeing scored cards is unknown.
147	
148	**Validation method:** Self-observation (≤5 real sessions).
149	Log every "I want to know X and the UI can't tell me" moment. Categorise each gap. If no moments: kill. If gaps are confirmed: check whether a sort/filter control solves it cheaper than a chat UI.
150	
151	**Confidence target:** Raise from 1 → 5 minimum before any build work. Natural language requires evidence beyond "comparison need exists."
152	
153	**Current ICE:** 6 × 1 × 2 = 12.
154	
155	---
156	
157	## Shape review
158	
159	| Pattern | Status | Notes |
160	|---|---|---|
161	| Customer-specials dominance | ok | Personal tool; no external stakeholders. `cs` = 0%. |
162	| Tech foundation starvation | flagged | `tech` at 5%. Rails monolith appears healthy; photo analyzer disabled but not blocking. Revisit if KTLO grows above 20%. |
163	| Differentiators at low confidence | ok | Only `diff` in a build slot is shareable shortlist (Confidence 5 = threshold). Neighbourhood RAG (`diff`) correctly in validation. |
164	| No speculative bets | ok | Q&A agent (`spec`) in validation. Intentional — confidence too low for a build bet. |
165	| Must-be gaps unfixed | ok | Shareable shortlist (Kano Must-be) is in Now. No other Must-be gaps identified. |
166	| Validation items in build slots | ok | Push notifications (2), Neighbourhood RAG (2), Q&A agent (1) all in validation slots. ✓ |
167

id=\"shareable-shortlist\">Shareable shortlist — partner reactions and notes</h3>\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-problem\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\">Problem</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\"><p>For a two-person household evaluating Amsterdam rentals remotely, we believe the absence of a shared view into the scored shortlist is causing joint listing decisions to be made via screenshots and messaging apps rather than within the tool, fragmenting the evaluation workflow and preventing the partner from participating with structured reactions or notes.</p></div>\n</section>\n<p><strong>Success criterion:</strong> Partner accesses the shared shortlist URL and leaves at least one reaction or note on a listing within 72 hours of the first share event.</p>\n<p><strong>Appetite:</strong> 2 weeks. Cap, not estimate. If full scope (reactions + notes + combined status) doesn't fit, cut to: share URL → partner read-only view → like/dislike reaction. Notes and combined status move to Next.</p>\n<p><strong>Theme:</strong> <span class=\"p-theme\">diff</span> — no Dutch rental platform supports partner co-review without accounts.</p>\n<p><strong>ICE:</strong> 8 × 5 × 4 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">160</span></p>\n<p><strong>Kano:</strong> Must-be. Without it, the partner cannot participate. Absence blocks the core household workflow.</p>\n\n<h2 id=\"next\">Next</h2>\n<h3 id=\"daily-digest-email\">Daily digest email — Melbourne morning timing</h3>\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-problem\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\">Problem</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\"><p>For an expat user in the Melbourne timezone checking Amsterdam listings, we believe the absence of a proactive daily summary delivered at the right local time is causing the app to fail its \"works while you sleep\" promise: new listings require active app-checking rather than arriving in the user's inbox when they are ready to act.</p></div>\n</section>\n<p><strong>Theme:</strong> <span class=\"p-theme\">incr</span></p>\n<p><strong>ICE:</strong> 6 × 5 × 7 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"green\">210</span></p>\n<p><strong>Note:</strong> Sequence after push notification validation resolves. If push notifications are validated and built, design digest and push together to avoid notification fatigue (a listing shouldn't appear in both a push alert and the same morning's digest). If push is killed, digest ships standalone.</p>\n\n<h2 id=\"later\">Later</h2>\n<p>These are strategic hypotheses, not commitments. No scoping until they move to Next.</p>\n<h3>Map view with listing locations and cycling routes</h3>\n<p>Helps users orient listings spatially. No confirmed gap in current tool — users may not need it if scores are sufficient.</p>\n<h3>Pararius / HousingAnywhere adapters</h3>\n<p>Expands coverage beyond Funda. Priority rises if Funda coverage proves insufficient or listings become scarce.</p>\n<h3>Score overrides after viewings</h3>\n<p>Post-viewing corrections to provisional scores. Low priority until listings are being visited in person.</p>\n<h3>Proximity scoring (parks, supermarkets, transit via Places API)</h3>\n<p>Enriches the Neighbourhood Vibe section with data. Complements — and possibly supersedes — the Neighbourhood RAG validation bet.</p>\n\n<h2 id=\"validation-slots\">Validation slots</h2>\n\n<h3 id=\"push-notifications\">Push notifications — high-scoring listings</h3>\n<p><strong>Hypothesis:</strong> Proactive alerts for high-scoring listings would reduce the window between ingestion and user awareness, reducing the risk of missing a listing before acting on it.</p>\n<p><strong>Why it's here:</strong> Manual checking habit confirmed in PRD. But the platform already has a near-real-time ingestion path via forwarded email alerts. The user-awareness gap (listing ingested → user notified) may not exist or may already be closed. Problem is plausible but not confirmed.</p>\n<p><strong>Validation method:</strong> Gap audit (≤2 hours, no code).</p>\n<ol>\n  <li>Review last 2–4 weeks of ingested listings. For each high-scoring listing: when ingested vs. when first viewed. Is there a consistent multi-hour delay?</li>\n  <li>Trace the Funda email alert → forwarded → ingestion flow. Does ingestion already produce a visible user-facing signal?</li>\n  <li>If gap is confirmed: assess whether a triggered mailer (easy, ~1 day) closes it before committing to web push infrastructure.</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>Confidence target:</strong> Raise from <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">2</span> → <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">6</span>. If gap audit shows no meaningful delay: kill or defer. If confirmed: move to idea bank and schedule after digest email ships.</p>\n<p><strong>Current ICE:</strong> 7 × 2 × 6 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">84</span>. If validated: projected 7 × 6 × 6 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"green\">252</span>.</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"neighbourhood-rag\">Neighbourhood RAG — \"About this area\" panel</h3>\n<p><strong>Hypothesis:</strong> An AI-generated neighbourhood brief (free text + concerns + citations) would replace the user's manual Reddit/Google research step per listing, accelerating the shortlist-to-viewing-decision workflow.</p>\n<p><strong>Why it's here:</strong> Problem confirmed (manual step exists). Solution unvalidated — Dutch-language source coverage, RAG quality for hyper-local facts, and hallucination risk are all unknown.</p>\n<p><strong>Validation method:</strong> 1-day spike.</p>\n<ol>\n  <li>Pick 3–5 Amsterdam neighbourhoods (one well-reviewed, one contested, one obscure).</li>\n  <li>Fetch top Reddit/Google results manually for each.</li>\n  <li>Prompt Claude: generate free-text brief + concerns list + citations from that content.</li>\n  <li>Evaluate: Is the output accurate? Does it surface real concerns? Would you trust it over doing the research yourself?</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>Confidence target:</strong> Raise from <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">2</span> → <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">6</span>. If spike output is not trustworthy: kill or reshape as curated-links fallback (lower impact, no hallucination risk).</p>\n<p><strong>Current ICE:</strong> 7 × 2 × 3 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">42</span>. If validated: projected 7 × 7 × 3 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">147</span>.</p>\n\n<h3 id=\"qa-agent\">Q&amp;A agent — shortlist chat</h3>\n<p><strong>Hypothesis:</strong> A natural-language chat interface on the shortlist would let users compare listings and ask cross-listing questions faster than reading individual cards.</p>\n<p><strong>Why it's here:</strong> Problem unconfirmed. The tool already answers \"which listings are worth my time\" via scoring and ranking. Whether residual unanswered questions exist after seeing scored cards is unknown.</p>\n<p><strong>Validation method:</strong> Self-observation (≤5 real sessions). Log every \"I want to know X and the UI can't tell me\" moment. Categorise each gap. If no moments: kill. If gaps are confirmed: check whether a sort/filter control solves it cheaper than a chat UI.</p>\n<p><strong>Confidence target:</strong> Raise from <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">1</span> → <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">5</span> minimum before any build work. Natural language requires evidence beyond \"comparison need exists.\"</p>\n<p><strong>Current ICE:</strong> 6 × 1 × 2 = <span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">12</span>.</p>\n\n<h2 id=\"shape-review\">Shape review</h2>\n<table>\n  <thead><tr><th>Pattern</th><th>Status</th><th>Notes</th></tr></thead>\n  <tbody>\n    <tr><td>Customer-specials dominance</td><td>ok</td><td>Personal tool; no external stakeholders. <code>cs</code> = 0%.</td></tr>\n    <tr><td>Tech foundation starvation</td><td>flagged</td><td><code>tech</code> at 5%. Rails monolith appears healthy; photo analyzer disabled but not blocking. Revisit if KTLO grows above 20%.</td></tr>\n    <tr><td>Differentiators at low confidence</td><td>ok</td><td>Only <code>diff</code> in a build slot is shareable shortlist (Confidence 5 = threshold). Neighbourhood RAG (<code>diff</code>) correctly in validation.</td></tr>\n    <tr><td>No speculative bets</td><td>ok</td><td>Q&amp;A agent (<code>spec</code>) in validation. Intentional — confidence too low for a build bet.</td></tr>\n    <tr><td>Must-be gaps unfixed</td><td>ok</td><td>Shareable shortlist (Kano Must-be) is in Now. No other Must-be gaps identified.</td></tr>\n    <tr><td>Validation items in build slots</td><td>ok</td><td>Push notifications (2), Neighbourhood RAG (2), Q&amp;A agent (1) all in validation slots. ✓</td></tr>\n  </tbody>\n</table>\n\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"

1	# ADR-001: Ruby on Rails Monolith
2	
3	**Status:** Accepted
4	**Date:** 31 March 2026
5	
6	## Context
7	
8	We need to choose a tech stack for Nestl, a single-user rental listing evaluator. The app has a standard web UI (listing feed, detail pages, forms), background job processing (scraping, email ingestion), and no complex client-side interactivity. It will be built and maintained by a solo developer.
9	
10	## Decision
11	
12	Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.
13	
14	## Rationale
15	
16	**Why Rails:**
17	- Full-stack framework: models, views, controllers, jobs, mailers, email ingestion (Action Mailbox) all in one codebase
18	- Rails 8 defaults (SQLite, Solid Queue, Solid Cache, Solid Cable, Kamal) eliminate external dependencies — no Redis, no Postgres, no separate job runner
19	- Mature scraping ecosystem (Nokogiri is bundled, HTTP.rb for HTTP client)
20	- Convention-over-configuration means less decision fatigue for a solo developer
21	- The app is fundamentally CRUD + background jobs + server-rendered pages — Rails' sweet spot
22	
23	**Why not a separate frontend (React/Next.js):**
24	- The UI is listing cards, detail pages, and forms — no complex client-side state
25	- A separate frontend doubles the deployment surface, adds a build pipeline, and introduces API serialisation overhead
26	- Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus) handles the few dynamic interactions needed (shortlist actions, filters) without a JS framework
27	
28	**Why not Python/FastAPI:**
29	- FastAPI is backend-only; would still need a frontend framework or templating layer
30	- Django would be comparable to Rails but the developer prefers Ruby
31	
32	**Why SQLite over Postgres:**
33	- Single-user app with low write volume (tens of listings per day)
34	- Rails 8 has first-class SQLite support including for queues, cache, and WebSockets
35	- Zero-ops: no database server to manage
36	- Can migrate to Postgres later if multi-user support is added
37	
38	## Consequences
39	
40	- All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit
41	- Background jobs (scraping, email processing, evaluation) run in the same process via Solid Queue
42	- Deployment is a single Docker container via Kamal to a VPS
43	- If we later need a separate API (e.g. for a native mobile app), we can add API controllers to the same Rails app
44	
```

padding-left: 22px; }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div class=\"layout\">\n<nav class=\"toc\" aria-label=\"Table of contents\">\n<h2>Contents</h2>\n<ul>\n  <li><a href=\"#context\">Context</a></li>\n  <li><a href=\"#decision\">Decision</a></li>\n  <li><a href=\"#rationale\">Rationale</a></li>\n  <li><a href=\"#consequences\">Consequences</a></li>\n</ul>\n</nav>\n<main>\n<h1>ADR-001: Ruby on Rails Monolith</h1>\n<p class=\"meta\">Rendered from <code>docs/adr/001-rails-monolith.md</code> on 2026-05-17</p>\n<div class=\"status-bar\">\n  <span><strong>Status:</strong> <span class=\"status-pill\">Accepted</span></span>\n  <span><strong>Date:</strong> 31 March 2026</span>\n</div>\n\n<h2 id=\"context\">Context</h2>\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-context\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\">Context</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\">\n    <p>We need to choose a tech stack for Nestl, a single-user rental listing evaluator. The app has a standard web UI (listing feed, detail pages, forms), background job processing (scraping, email ingestion), and no complex client-side interactivity. It will be built and maintained by a solo developer.</p>\n  </div>\n</section>\n\n<h2 id=\"decision\">Decision</h2>\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-decision\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\">Decision</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\">\n    <p>Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.</p>\n  </div>\n</section>\n\n<h2 id=\"rationale\">Rationale</h2>\n\n<div class=\"rationale-block\">\n  <h4>Why Rails</h4>\n  <ul>\n    <li>Full-stack framework: models, views, controllers, jobs, mailers, email ingestion (Action Mailbox) all in one codebase</li>\n    <li>Rails 8 defaults (SQLite, Solid Queue, Solid Cache, Solid Cable, Kamal) eliminate external dependencies — no Redis, no Postgres, no separate job runner</li>\n    <li>Mature scraping ecosystem (Nokogiri is bundled, HTTP.rb for HTTP client)</li>\n    <li>Convention-over-configuration means less decision fatigue for a solo developer</li>\n    <li>The app is fundamentally CRUD + background jobs + server-rendered pages — Rails' sweet spot</li>\n  </ul>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"rationale-block\">\n  <h4>Why not a separate frontend (React/Next.js)</h4>\n  <ul>\n    <li>The UI is listing cards, detail pages, and forms — no complex client-side state</li>\n    <li>A separate frontend doubles the deployment surface, adds a build pipeline, and introduces API serialisation overhead</li>\n    <li>Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus) handles the few dynamic interactions needed (shortlist actions, filters) without a JS framework</li>\n  </ul>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"rationale-block\">\n  <h4>Why not Python/FastAPI</h4>\n  <ul>\n    <li>FastAPI is backend-only; would still need a frontend framework or templating layer</li>\n    <li>Django would be comparable to Rails but the developer prefers Ruby</li>\n  </ul>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"rationale-block\">\n  <h4>Why SQLite over Postgres</h4>\n  <ul>\n    <li>Single-user app with low write volume (tens of listings per day)</li>\n    <li>Rails 8 has first-class SQLite support including for queues, cache, and WebSockets</li>\n    <li>Zero-ops: no database server to manage</li>\n    <li>Can migrate to Postgres later if multi-user support is added</li>\n  </ul>\n</div>\n\n<h2 id=\"consequences\">Consequences</h2>\n<section class=\"p-callout p-callout-consequences\">\n  <header class=\"p-callout-head\">Consequences</header>\n  <div class=\"p-callout-body\">\n    <ul>\n      <li>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</li>\n      <li>Background jobs (scraping, email processing, evaluation) run in the same process via Solid Queue</li>\n      <li>Deployment is a single Docker container via Kamal to a VPS</li>\n      <li>If we later need a separate API (e.g. for a native mobile app), we can add API controllers to the same Rails app</li>\n    </ul>\n  </div>\n</section>\n\n</main>\n<footer>Generated by render-html. Re-render the source markdown to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('nav.toc a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
}
