1	---
2	name: html-patterns
3	description: >
4	  Pattern catalogue for the render-html skill. Each entry maps a recognisable
5	  markdown shape to a richer HTML treatment — side-by-side columns, kanban,
6	  timelines, callouts, score chips. The skill consults this file during
7	  conversion (Step 4) and substitutes the matching markup into {{body-html}}.
8	type: reference
9	cited_by:
10	  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md
11	---
12	
13	# HTML pattern catalogue
14	
15	The render-html skill walks the markdown body section by section. For each section, it checks the patterns below in order. The **first match wins** — if no pattern matches, the section renders as plain prose (the walking-skeleton fallback).
16	
17	Every pattern is defined by:
18	
19	1. **Detect** — the markdown signal that triggers the treatment. Cheap to check; conservative (false-negative is fine, false-positive is bad — wrongly-treated content is harder to debug than untreated).
20	2. **HTML** — the output markup. All class names are prefixed `p-` so they don't collide with prose styles.
21	3. **CSS** — additions appended to the shell's inline `<style>` block. The shell's prose styles remain the fallback; pattern CSS only adds, never overrides.
22	
23	The hard self-containment rule from `html-skeleton.md` applies to every pattern: no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. Inline SVG is preferred for visual elements.
24	
25	---
26	
27	## P1 — Alternatives considered → side-by-side columns
28	
29	**Detect.** A section whose heading text matches `/alternatives?( considered)?/i`, OR a section containing two-to-four sibling H3s each followed by a bulleted list whose first item starts with `Pros:` or `Cons:` (case-insensitive). Maximum four columns — beyond that, fall back to prose (columns become unreadable). **Does not match** ADR `Rationale` sections whose options use bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**`) rather than H3 — those go to P11.
30	
31	**HTML.**
32	
33	```html
34	<section class="p-alternatives">
35	  <div class="p-alt-col">
36	    <h3>Alternative A — short name</h3>
37	    <dl>
38	      <dt>Pros</dt><dd><ul><li>...</li></ul></dd>
39	      <dt>Cons</dt><dd><ul><li>...</li></ul></dd>
40	      <dt>Cost</dt><dd>...</dd>
41	      <dt>Reversibility</dt><dd>...</dd>
42	    </dl>
43	  </div>
44	  <!-- repeat per alternative -->
45	</section>
46	```
47	
48	**CSS.**
49	
50	```css
51	.p-alternatives { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(220px, 1fr)); gap: 16px; margin: 16px 0; }
52	.p-alt-col { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 14px 16px; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.03); }
53	.p-alt-col h3 { margin-top: 0; font-size: 15px; }
54	.p-alt-col dl { margin: 0; }
55	.p-alt-col dt { font-size: 11px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 10px; }
56	.p-alt-col dd { margin: 4px 0 0; font-size: 14px; }
57	.p-alt-col ul { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; }
58	```
59	
60	**Accessibility.** Each column is a `<section>` with a unique `<h3>`; the columns still appear in DOM order, so screen readers read them sequentially. No keyboard interaction needed — pure layout.
61	
62	---
63	
64	## P2 — Task list with dependencies → vertical timeline
65	
66	**Detect.** A section containing an ordered list (or sequence of H3s named `Task N — title` / `Step N`) where two or more entries mention "depends on", "after", "blocks", or "blocked by" in their first paragraph. Pure task lists without dependency language stay as ordinary ordered lists.
67	
68	**HTML.**
69	
70	```html
71	<ol class="p-timeline">
72	  <li class="p-task" id="task-1">
73	    <div class="p-task-marker" aria-hidden="true">1</div>
74	    <div class="p-task-body">
75	      <h3>Task 1 — name</h3>
76	      <p class="p-task-deps">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>
77	      <p>Task description prose…</p>
78	    </div>
79	  </li>
80	  <!-- repeat per task; dependency lines link to #task-N -->
81	</ol>
82	```
83	
84	**CSS.**
85	
86	```css
87	.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 16px 0; position: relative; }
88	.p-timeline::before { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 14px; top: 0; bottom: 0; width: 2px; background: var(--rule); }
89	.p-task { position: relative; padding-left: 44px; margin-bottom: 18px; }
90	.p-task-marker { position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; width: 30px; height: 30px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--bg); border: 2px solid var(--accent); color: var(--accent); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; font-weight: 600; font-size: 13px; }
91	.p-task-body h3 { margin: 4px 0 6px; font-size: 16px; }
92	.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13px; margin: 0 0 8px; }
93	.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }
94	```
95	
96	**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href="#task-2">Task 2</a>`); marker is `aria-hidden`.
97	
98	---
99	
100	## P3 — Design-doc blocks → coloured callouts
101	
102	**Detect.** H2 headings whose text matches one of: `Problem`, `Context`, `Constraints`, `Decision` (or `Recommended approach`), `Consequences`, `Open questions`. Apply per matching H2 — sibling non-matching H2s render as prose.
103	
104	**HTML.**
105	
106	```html
107	<section class="p-callout p-callout-problem">
108	  <header class="p-callout-head"><span class="p-callout-icon" aria-hidden="true">▶</span> Problem</header>
109	  <div class="p-callout-body">
110	    <!-- prose -->
111	  </div>
112	</section>
113	```
114	
115	Mapping (used for `p-callout-<key>`): `problem` → red, `context` → grey, `constraints` → amber, `decision` → blue, `consequences` → green, `open-questions` → purple.
116	
117	**CSS.**
118	
119	```css
120	.p-callout { margin: 20px 0; border-left: 4px solid var(--cb, var(--rule)); border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; background: var(--cbg, rgba(127,127,127,0.04)); padding: 12px 16px; }
121	.p-callout-head { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--cb, var(--muted)); margin-bottom: 6px; font-weight: 600; }
122	.p-callout-icon { display: inline-block; margin-right: 4px; }
123	.p-callout-body > :first-child { margin-top: 0; }
124	.p-callout-body > :last-child { margin-bottom: 0; }
125	.p-callout-problem      { --cb: #dc3545; --cbg: rgba(220,53,69,0.06); }
126	.p-callout-context      { --cb: #6c757d; --cbg: rgba(108,117,125,0.06); }
127	.p-callout-constraints  { --cb: #fd7e14; --cbg: rgba(253,126,20,0.07); }
128	.p-callout-decision     { --cb: #0a58ca; --cbg: rgba(10,88,202,0.06); }
129	.p-callout-consequences { --cb: #198754; --cbg: rgba(25,135,84,0.06); }
130	.p-callout-open-questions { --cb: #6f42c1; --cbg: rgba(111,66,193,0.06); }
131	```
132	
133	**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the heading text carries the meaning. Border + label work for users who can't perceive the hue.
134	
135	**Sub-buckets inside `Consequences`.** When a `Consequences` callout contains H3s (or bold-paragraph headings) named `Positive`, `Negative`, `Neutral` / `Open`, render each as a coloured sub-block inside the callout rather than as nested H3s. Sub-block CSS:
136	
137	```css
138	.p-conseq { margin: 12px 0; padding: 10px 14px; border-radius: 6px; }
139	.p-conseq-positive { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.05);  border-left: 3px solid #198754; }
140	.p-conseq-negative { background: rgba(220,53,69,0.05);  border-left: 3px solid #dc3545; }
141	.p-conseq-neutral  { background: rgba(108,117,125,0.05); border-left: 3px solid #6c757d; }
142	```
143	
144	This is a P3 extension, not a separate pattern — the parent callout is still `p-callout-consequences`.
145	
146	---
147	
148	## P4 — NFRs table → highlighted numeric table
149	
150	**Detect.** Markdown table inside a section whose heading matches `/non-functional|NFRs?/i`, OR any table whose header row contains one of: `Target`, `SLO`, `p95`, `p99`, `Latency`, `Throughput`, `Availability`, `Budget`.
151	
152	**HTML.** Standard `<table>` with `class="p-nfr"`; cells that parse as numbers (optionally with units `ms`, `s`, `%`, `/s`, `KB`, `MB`) get `class="p-num"`.
153	
154	```html
155	<table class="p-nfr">
156	  <thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>Target</th><th>Current</th></tr></thead>
157	  <tbody>
158	    <tr><td>p95 latency</td><td class="p-num">200 ms</td><td class="p-num">340 ms</td></tr>
159	  </tbody>
160	</table>
161	```
162	
163	**CSS.**
164	
165	```css
166	.p-nfr { width: 100%; }
167	.p-nfr th { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.06); font-size: 13px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.03em; color: var(--muted); }
168	.p-nfr td.p-num { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; }
169	.p-nfr tbody tr:hover { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.04); }
170	```
171	
172	**Accessibility.** Standard table semantics; `<thead>`/`<tbody>` preserved.
173	
174	---
175	
176	## P5 — Open questions → checklist with owner / due
177	
178	**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?/i`. Body is a bullet list; each bullet may end with parenthetical metadata `(owner: name, due: 2026-06-01)` or markdown task-list syntax `- [ ]` / `- [x]`.
179	
180	**HTML.**
181	
182	```html
183	<ul class="p-questions">
184	  <li class="p-q">
185	    <input type="checkbox" disabled> <!-- checked if [x] -->
186	    <span class="p-q-text">Should we use Postgres or DynamoDB for the audit log?</span>
187	    <span class="p-q-meta">@anton · due 2026-06-01</span>
188	  </li>
189	</ul>
190	```
191	
192	**CSS.**
193	
194	```css
195	.p-questions { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 16px 0; }
196	.p-q { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto; gap: 10px; align-items: baseline; padding: 8px 0; border-bottom: 1px dashed var(--rule); }
197	.p-q input[type=checkbox] { transform: translateY(2px); }
198	.p-q-text { color: var(--fg); }
199	.p-q-meta { color: var(--muted); font-size: 12px; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }
200	```
201	
202	**Accessibility.** The checkbox is `disabled` (read-only display); the parenthetical metadata is preserved as visible text, not buried in `title=` attributes.
203	
204	---
205	
206	## P6 — Diff / before-after → two-column annotated diff
207	
208	**Detect.** Two adjacent fenced code blocks within the same H3 / paragraph where the first is labelled (in info string or preceding line) `before`, `was`, `old`, `current`, and the second `after`, `now`, `new`, `proposed`. Also: any code block with info string `diff` renders inline with `+`/`-` line colouring.
209	
210	**HTML (two-column).**
211	
212	```html
213	<div class="p-diff">
214	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-before">
215	    <header>Before</header>
216	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
217	  </div>
218	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-after">
219	    <header>After</header>
220	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
221	  </div>
222	</div>
223	```
224	
225	**HTML (inline diff).** Standard `<pre><code class="language-diff">` with each line wrapped: `<span class="p-diff-add">+ …</span>`, `<span class="p-diff-del">- …</span>`.
226	
227	**CSS.**
228	
229	```css
230	.p-diff { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 12px; margin: 16px 0; }
231	.p-diff-side header { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 4px; }
232	.p-diff-before pre { border-left: 3px solid #dc3545; }
233	.p-diff-after  pre { border-left: 3px solid #198754; }
234	.p-diff-add { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.12); display: block; }
235	.p-diff-del { background: rgba(220,53,69,0.12); display: block; }
236	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-diff { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
237	```
238	
239	**Accessibility.** Headers (`Before` / `After`) carry the meaning; colour stripe is supplemental.
240	
241	---
242	
243	## P7 — Roadmap Now / Next / Later → three-column kanban
244	
245	**Detect.** Three sibling H2s or H3s with text exactly matching (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant): `Now`, `Next`, `Later`, **AND** each of those sections' body is predominantly a single `<ul>`/`<ol>` (no nested H3s, no Problem/Appetite/ICE callout sub-structure). If only two of the three columns are present and the bullet-only condition holds, still render as a kanban with the missing column shown empty. If the bullet-only condition fails (sections contain rich prose, callouts, or H3 sub-items per column), **do not apply** — fall back to prose so the column content stays readable. Evidence: collapsing a prose-heavy "Now" section into bullets destroys the Problem / Success criterion / Appetite / ICE structure roadmap items typically carry.
246	
247	**HTML.**
248	
249	```html
250	<div class="p-kanban">
251	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="now">
252	    <header>Now</header>
253	    <ul>
254	      <li>Item one</li>
255	      <li>Item two</li>
256	    </ul>
257	  </div>
258	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="next"> … </div>
259	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="later"> … </div>
260	</div>
261	```
262	
263	**CSS.**
264	
265	```css
266	.p-kanban { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 12px; margin: 20px 0; }
267	.p-kanban-col { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; padding: 12px 14px; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.03); min-height: 120px; }
268	.p-kanban-col header { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.05em; color: var(--muted); padding-bottom: 8px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); margin-bottom: 10px; font-weight: 600; }
269	.p-kanban-col[data-col="now"]   header { color: #198754; }
270	.p-kanban-col[data-col="next"]  header { color: #0a58ca; }
271	.p-kanban-col[data-col="later"] header { color: #6c757d; }
272	.p-kanban-col ul { padding-left: 18px; margin: 0; font-size: 14px; }
273	.p-kanban-col li { margin: 6px 0; }
274	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-kanban { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
275	```
276	
277	**Accessibility.** Each column is keyboard-navigable as ordinary text; the header colour is supplemental to the label.
278	
279	---
280	
281	## P8 — ICE scores → coloured score chips
282	
283	**Detect.** Markdown table whose header row contains EITHER (a) `Impact`, `Confidence`, and `Ease` (in any order, plus an optional `Score` column), OR (b) a single `ICE` column alongside at least one of `Confidence` / `Slot` / `Theme` / `Kano` — real roadmaps frequently collapse the three sub-scores into one `ICE` total rather than listing each. Numeric cells are graded on the same red/amber/green band regardless of column. Em-dash placeholders (`—`) render as a muted chip.
284	
285	**HTML.** Numeric cells become chips coloured by value (red 1–3, amber 4–6, green 7–10).
286	
287	```html
288	<table class="p-ice">
289	  <thead><tr><th>Idea</th><th>Impact</th><th>Confidence</th><th>Ease</th><th>ICE</th></tr></thead>
290	  <tbody>
291	    <tr>
292	      <td>Onboarding revamp</td>
293	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="green">9</span></td>
294	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="amber">5</span></td>
295	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="red">3</span></td>
296	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="amber">135</span></td>
297	    </tr>
298	  </tbody>
299	</table>
300	```
301	
302	**CSS.**
303	
304	```css
305	.p-ice .p-chip { display: inline-block; min-width: 28px; padding: 2px 8px; border-radius: 999px; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 12px; text-align: center; font-weight: 600; }
306	.p-chip[data-band="red"]   { background: rgba(220,53,69,0.18); color: #dc3545; }
307	.p-chip[data-band="amber"] { background: rgba(253,126,20,0.18); color: #fd7e14; }
308	.p-chip[data-band="green"] { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.18); color: #198754; }
309	.p-chip[data-band="muted"] { background: rgba(127,127,127,0.12); color: var(--muted); }
310	.p-ice td:not(:first-child) { text-align: center; }
311	```
312	
313	Chip bands: `1–3 → red`, `4–6 → amber`, `7–10 → green`, em-dash / missing → `muted`. ICE totals (any value above 10) are graded against the same 50 / 100 / 200 thresholds used for sequencing in roadmaps that follow `PRODUCT_RULES`.
314	
315	**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the numeric value is the source of truth. Chips render as plain numbers in print and to screen readers.
316	
317	---
318	
319	## P9 — YAML frontmatter → metadata grid
320	
321	**Detect.** Source markdown begins with a YAML frontmatter block delimited by `---` on its own line, before the H1. Common in ADRs and design docs (fields like `id`, `status`, `date`, `authors`, `linear`, `supersedes`).
322	
323	**HTML.** Render as a definition list immediately under the page title and the provenance meta line — before any body content.
324	
325	```html
326	<dl class="p-frontmatter">
327	  <dt>id</dt><dd>ADR-engagement-modifier-constants</dd>
328	  <dt>status</dt><dd><span class="p-status p-status-accepted">accepted</span></dd>
329	  <dt>date</dt><dd>2026-05-14</dd>
330	  <dt>authors</dt><dd>Anton Babushkin</dd>
331	</dl>
332	```
333	
334	**CSS.**
335	
336	```css
337	.p-frontmatter { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr; gap: 4px 14px; font-size: 13px; padding: 12px 14px; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.04); border-radius: 6px; margin-bottom: 24px; }
338	.p-frontmatter dt { color: var(--muted); text-transform: uppercase; font-size: 11px; letter-spacing: 0.04em; margin: 0; }
339	.p-frontmatter dd { margin: 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 12.5px; }
340	```
341	
342	The `status` field gets the P10 status-pill treatment automatically; other field values render as plain text. **Do not** drop frontmatter on the floor — readers use it to anchor the doc.
343	
344	**Accessibility.** Definition list is the right semantic tag for key-value metadata; screen readers announce each pair.
345	
346	---
347	
348	## P10 — Status / state values → coloured pill
349	
350	**Detect.** A frontmatter `status:` field, OR a top-of-doc bold line matching `/\*\*Status:\*\*\s+(\w+)/i`, OR a single-cell `Status` column in a table. Recognised values (case-insensitive): `accepted`, `proposed`, `draft`, `rejected`, `superseded`, `deprecated`, `flagged`, `ok`.
351	
352	**HTML.**
353	
354	```html
355	<span class="p-status p-status-accepted">Accepted</span>
356	```
357	
358	**CSS.**
359	
360	```css
361	.p-status { display: inline-block; padding: 2px 9px; border-radius: 999px; font-weight: 600; font-size: 11px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.04em; }
362	.p-status-accepted, .p-status-ok        { background: rgba(25,135,84,0.18);  color: #198754; }
363	.p-status-proposed, .p-status-draft     { background: rgba(10,88,202,0.18);  color: #0a58ca; }
364	.p-status-rejected, .p-status-deprecated{ background: rgba(220,53,69,0.18);  color: #dc3545; }
365	.p-status-superseded, .p-status-flagged { background: rgba(253,126,20,0.18); color: #fd7e14; }
366	```
367	
368	A pill is reusable wherever a state lives in the source — Slot columns (Now/Next/Later/Validation) in P7-rejected roadmap tables are a natural reuse site.
369	
370	**Accessibility.** The label text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.
371	
372	---
373	
374	## P11 — Rationale "Why X" blocks → labelled list groups
375	
376	**Detect.** A section heading matches `/rationale/i` AND its body contains two-or-more bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**` / `**Why not Y:**`) each followed by a bullet list. Common in ADRs that consider multiple alternatives without dedicated H3s per option.
377	
378	**HTML.** Each bold paragraph becomes the heading of a styled group; the following list is the group's body.
379	
380	```html
381	<section class="p-rationale">
382	  <div class="p-rationale-group">
383	    <h4>Why Rails</h4>
384	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
385	  </div>
386	  <div class="p-rationale-group">
387	    <h4>Why not a separate frontend</h4>
388	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
389	  </div>
390	</section>
391	```
392	
393	**CSS.**
394	
395	```css
396	.p-rationale-group { margin: 14px 0; }
397	.p-rationale-group h4 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 14px; color: var(--accent); font-weight: 600; }
398	.p-rationale-group ul { margin: 0; padding-left: 22px; }
399	```
400	
401	**Precedence.** P11 ranks below P1 (Alternatives). When both could match (`Rationale` section whose options also have `Pros:`/`Cons:`), P1's side-by-side comparison is the stronger treatment.
402	
403	**Accessibility.** `<h4>` keeps the headings in the document outline; lists keep list semantics.
404	
405	---
406	
407	## P12 — Labelled rubric cards (C1, C2, FR3 …) → repeated sub-field cards
408	
409	**Detect.** Two or more sibling H3s whose text matches `/^([A-Z]+\d+)\s+[—–-]\s+/` (e.g. `C1 — Deadband: ±2%`, `FR3 — Idempotent writes`, `OQ5 — …`), AND each section repeats the same set of bold-paragraph sub-fields (e.g. **Reasoning**, **Alternative considered**, **Revision trigger**). The identifier prefix (`C`, `FR`, `OQ`, `NFR`, `ADR`) is rendered as a pill on the card header.
410	
411	**HTML.**
412	
413	```html
414	<div class="p-rubric" id="c1">
415	  <h3><span class="p-rubric-id">C1</span> Deadband: ±2% YoY</h3>
416	  <div class="p-rubric-formula">If <code>abs(yoy_change) &lt; 0.02</code>, set <code>direction = 0</code> …</div>
417	  <h4>Reasoning</h4><p>…</p>
418	  <h4>Alternative considered</h4><p>…</p>
419	  <h4>Revision trigger</h4><p>…</p>
420	</div>
421	```
422	
423	The first paragraph (often a one-line formal definition or formula) becomes `p-rubric-formula`. Subsequent bold-paragraph sub-fields become `<h4>` headings inside the card.
424	
425	**CSS.**
426	
427	```css
428	.p-rubric { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 6px; padding: 14px 18px; margin: 16px 0; background: rgba(127,127,127,0.02); }
429	.p-rubric h3 { margin-top: 0; display: flex; gap: 12px; align-items: baseline; color: var(--fg); }
430	.p-rubric h3 .p-rubric-id { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 13px; padding: 2px 8px; background: var(--accent); color: white; border-radius: 4px; }
431	.p-rubric h4 { font-size: 13px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.04em; color: var(--muted); margin: 12px 0 4px; font-weight: 600; }
432	.p-rubric-formula { background: rgba(10,88,202,0.06); border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); padding: 8px 12px; margin: 8px 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: 13px; }
433	```
434	
435	**Why a dedicated pattern.** Plain-prose rendering of a 6-card ADR section (C1–C6 with Reasoning / Alternative / Revision trigger each) produces a wall of repeating H3+H4 that the reader has to mentally segment. The card boundary plus the identifier pill makes "which constant am I reading" answerable at a glance — which is most of what reviewers want when scanning a pre-registered constants ADR.
436	
437	**Accessibility.** Heading levels (`<h3>` outer, `<h4>` inner) preserve the document outline; the card's visual boundary is decorative.
438	
439	---
440	
441	## Fallback
442	
443	A section that matches no pattern renders with the shell's standard prose styles (`<h2>`, `<h3>`, `<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, `<pre>`). Fallback is the default, not the exception — false-positive pattern matches degrade review trust faster than missing treatments.
444	
445	## Adding a new pattern
446	
447	When a new pattern earns its slot:
448	
449	1. Add a section here following the same six-part structure: heading, **Detect**, **HTML**, **CSS**, **Accessibility**, brief rationale.
450	2. Number it sequentially (`P13 — …`) so cross-references stay stable.
451	3. Confirm the CSS only adds new selectors prefixed `p-` — it must not override any rule in `html-skeleton.md`.
452	4. If the detection signal could overlap an existing pattern, document the precedence rule explicitly. The first-match-wins ordering of this file is the resolution mechanism.
453	
```

Defines the single-file\n  document template — the product-UI visual system (Inter sans, indigo accent,\n  numbered sidebar nav with scrollspy, pill + metadata-grid utilities, section\n  headers with index + tag, dark-mode auto, print stylesheet) — plus the inline\n  scrollspy JS and the hard self-containment rule.\ntype: reference\ncited_by:\n  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md\n---\n\n# HTML skeleton\n\nThe render-html skill wraps every markdown body in the shell below. The shell is the **only** mechanism by which output styling and navigation arrive — no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. That rule is load-bearing for the skill's \"opens via `file://` with no network\" guarantee; reviewers attach these files to emails and store them in private wikis where CDN URLs silently break.\n\nThe visual language is **product-UI**: Inter (system fallback), tight type, indigo accent, sidebar with numbered nav and scrollspy, status pills and metadata grids as first-class. Reads as a Linear/Stripe spec page rather than a markdown previewer. Pattern treatments (P1–P12) extend this language; they don't introduce a competing one.\n\n## The hard rule\n\nAnything injected into the rendered output must satisfy all three:\n\n- **No `<link rel=\"stylesheet\">`.** All CSS lives in the inline `<style>` block.\n- **No `<script src=...>`.** All JS lives in the inline `<script>` block at the end of `<body>`.\n- **No remote `<img>` URLs.** Inline SVG is preferred; base64 data URIs are allowed when an SVG is impractical. Remote URLs (`http:`, `https:`, protocol-relative `//`) are not.\n\nIf a future pattern needs richer interactivity (charts, diagrams, syntax highlighting), inline the minimal library or hand-write the SVG. Adding a CDN is not an option.\n\n## Substitution tokens\n\n| Token | Source | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `{{title}}` | The source markdown's first H1 | Goes in both `<title>` and the page `<h1>`. |\n| `{{breadcrumb}}` | Inferred from path (e.g. `docs/adr/001-...` → `Architecture · ADRs · ADR-001`) or empty | Optional; render empty `<div>` if nothing meaningful to show. |\n| `{{header-pills}}` | Status pill (P10) + identifier pill + date pill, in that order | Rendered into the `.pill-row` slot. Empty if none apply. |\n| `{{meta-grid}}` | Definition-list rows for `Source`, `Rendered`, plus any frontmatter fields after P9 lifts them | The grid is always rendered; if only `Source` and `Rendered` apply, it shows two rows. |\n| `{{toc-entries}}` | One `<li>` per H2 (and indented H3), with anchor href to the heading's slug | H1 is the title, not a TOC entry. Slugs are kebab-case, deduplicated `-2`, `-3`. |\n| `{{status-block}}` | One-line aside under \"Status\" kicker in the sidebar (e.g. \"Accepted 31 Mar 2026. No superseding ADR.\") | Empty `<div>` if no status info. |\n| `{{body-html}}` | The markdown body converted to HTML, with H2/H3 carrying `id=\"<slug>\"` and wrapped per `html-patterns.md` | Pattern markup substitutes here. |\n| `{{source-path}}` | Path of the input `.md` file, relative to the repo root if possible | Shown in the metadata grid and footer. |\n| `{{render-date}}` | ISO date (YYYY-MM-DD) of the render | Provenance only — re-renders on the same day produce no diff. |\n\n## The shell\n\n```html\n<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<title>{{title}}</title>\n<style>\n  :root {\n    --bg: #fcfcfd;\n    --surface: #ffffff;\n    --fg: #0a0a0f;\n    --fg-soft: #3a3a44;\n    --muted: #6b6b76;\n    --rule: #e8e8ee;\n    --rule-soft: #f1f1f5;\n    --accent: #5b4cff;\n    --accent-soft: #efedff;\n    --ok: #0f8a55;       --ok-soft: #e6f5ed;\n    --warn: #b25e09;     --warn-soft: #fbf0e0;\n    --info: #1f6feb;     --info-soft: #e7f0fd;\n    --danger: #c4344b;   --danger-soft: #fbe6ea;\n  }\n  @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {\n    :root {\n      --bg: #0b0b10; --surface: #15151c; --fg: #f4f4f7; --fg-soft: #c8c8d0; --muted: #8a8a96;\n      --rule: #25252e; --rule-soft: #1c1c24; --accent: #8b7cff; --accent-soft: #2a2548;\n      --ok: #4fc28a; --ok-soft: #1a2e25; --warn: #e08a3a; --warn-soft: #2f2418;\n      --info: #6ea8fe; --info-soft: #1a2538; --danger: #e87085; --danger-soft: #2c1820;\n    }\n  }\n  * { box-sizing: border-box; }\n  html, body { margin: 0; padding: 0; background: var(--bg); color: var(--fg); }\n  body {\n    font: 14.5px/1.6 \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, \"Segoe UI\", Roboto, sans-serif;\n    font-feature-settings: \"cv11\", \"ss01\", \"ss03\", \"kern\", \"liga\";\n    -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;\n    -moz-osx-font-smoothing: grayscale;\n  }\n  a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }\n  a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }\n  code, pre { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, \"JetBrains Mono\", Menlo, monospace; }\n\n  /* ---------- Layout ---------- */\n  .layout { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 260px 1fr; min-height: 100vh; max-width: 1200px; margin: 0 auto; }\n\n  /* ---------- Sidebar ---------- */\n  aside {\n    position: sticky; top: 0; align-self: start; height: 100vh; overflow-y: auto;\n    padding: 28px 22px; border-right: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--bg);\n  }\n  aside .brand { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; font-weight: 600; font-size: 13px; margin-bottom: 28px; color: var(--fg); }\n  aside .brand .dot { width: 18px; height: 18px; border-radius: 5px; background: linear-gradient(135deg, var(--accent), #b599ff); flex-shrink: 0; }\n  aside .kicker { font-size: 10.5px; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin: 24px 0 10px; font-weight: 600; }\n  aside .kicker:first-of-type { margin-top: 0; }\n  aside nav ol { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 0; counter-reset: nav; }\n  aside nav li { counter-increment: nav; margin: 2px 0; }\n  aside nav li.h3 { margin-left: 28px; }\n  aside nav a {\n    display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; padding: 7px 10px; border-radius: 6px;\n    color: var(--fg-soft); font-size: 13.5px; transition: background 0.12s, color 0.12s;\n  }\n  aside nav li:not(.h3) > a::before { content: counter(nav, decimal-leading-zero); font: 600 11px/1 ui-monospace, monospace; color: var(--muted); width: 18px; flex-shrink: 0; }\n  aside nav a:hover { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--fg); text-decoration: none; }\n  aside nav a.active { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }\n  aside nav a.active::before { color: var(--accent); }\n  aside .status-block { font-size: 12.5px; color: var(--muted); line-height: 1.5; }\n\n  /* ---------- Main ---------- */\n  main { padding: 40px 56px 80px; max-width: 860px; }\n\n  /* ---------- Document header ---------- */\n  .doc-head { margin-bottom: 36px; }\n  .breadcrumbs { font-size: 12.5px; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 14px; }\n  .breadcrumbs span { color: var(--fg-soft); }\n  main h1 { font-size: 30px; line-height: 1.2; margin: 0 0 14px; letter-spacing: -0.02em; font-weight: 700; }\n  .pill-row { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 8px; align-items: center; margin-bottom: 0; }\n  .pill-row:empty { display: none; }\n\n  /* ---------- Pills (utility, reusable) ---------- */\n  .pill {\n    display: inline-flex; align-items: center; gap: 6px; padding: 4px 10px; border-radius: 999px;\n    font: 600 11.5px/1 ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.01em; white-space: nowrap;\n  }\n  .pill .dot { width: 6px; height: 6px; border-radius: 50%; background: currentColor; }\n  .pill-meta     { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--fg-soft); font-weight: 500; }\n  .pill-accepted, .pill-ok        { background: var(--ok-soft);     color: var(--ok); }\n  .pill-proposed, .pill-draft     { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }\n  .pill-rejected, .pill-deprecated{ background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }\n  .pill-superseded, .pill-flagged { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }\n\n  /* ---------- Metadata grid (utility, reusable) ---------- */\n  .meta-grid {\n    display: grid; grid-template-columns: max-content 1fr; gap: 6px 18px;\n    background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px;\n    padding: 14px 18px; margin: 24px 0 0; font-size: 13px;\n  }\n  .meta-grid dt { color: var(--muted); font-weight: 500; }\n  .meta-grid dd { margin: 0; color: var(--fg); font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 12.5px; word-break: break-word; }\n\n  /* ---------- Section header (utility for pattern + prose alike) ---------- */\n  section.s { margin: 48px 0; scroll-margin-top: 20px; }\n  section.s > header { display: flex; align-items: baseline; gap: 10px; margin-bottom: 14px; flex-wrap: wrap; }\n  section.s .ix { font: 600 11px/1 ui-monospace, monospace; color: var(--muted); padding: 3px 7px; border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 4px; flex-shrink: 0; }\n  main h2 { font-size: 19px; line-height: 1.3; margin: 0; letter-spacing: -0.01em; font-weight: 650; }\n  main h3 { font-size: 15.5px; line-height: 1.4; margin: 24px 0 8px; font-weight: 600; }\n\n  /* ---------- Prose defaults ---------- */\n  main p { margin: 0 0 14px; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n  main ul, main ol { margin: 0 0 14px; padding-left: 24px; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n  main li { margin: 4px 0; }\n  main pre { background: var(--rule-soft); padding: 14px 16px; border-radius: 8px; overflow-x: auto; font-size: 12.5px; line-height: 1.55; border: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  main code { font-size: 0.92em; padding: 1px 5px; background: var(--rule-soft); border-radius: 3px; }\n  main pre code { padding: 0; background: transparent; }\n  main blockquote { margin: 16px 0; padding: 8px 16px; border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); color: var(--fg-soft); background: var(--accent-soft); border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; }\n  main table { border-collapse: collapse; margin: 14px 0; width: 100%; font-size: 13.5px; }\n  main th, main td { border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 8px 12px; text-align: left; }\n  main th { background: var(--rule-soft); font-weight: 600; color: var(--fg); font-size: 12.5px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.03em; }\n  main hr { border: none; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); margin: 32px 0; }\n\n  /* ---------- Footer ---------- */\n  footer { grid-column: 1 / -1; padding: 24px 56px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 12px; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  footer code { background: transparent; padding: 0; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n\n  /* ---------- Print ---------- */\n  @media print {\n    aside { display: none; }\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n    main { padding: 24px; max-width: none; }\n  }\n\n  /* ---------- Responsive ---------- */\n  @media (max-width: 880px) {\n    .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n    aside { position: static; height: auto; border-right: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 20px; }\n    aside nav ol { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 4px; }\n    aside .kicker { margin-top: 16px; }\n    main { padding: 28px 24px 60px; }\n  }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div class=\"layout\">\n  <aside>\n    <div class=\"brand\"><span class=\"dot\"></span><span>{{breadcrumb-short}}</span></div>\n    <p class=\"kicker\">On this page</p>\n    <nav aria-label=\"Table of contents\">\n      <ol>\n        {{toc-entries}}\n      </ol>\n    </nav>\n    <p class=\"kicker\">Status</p>\n    <div class=\"status-block\">{{status-block}}</div>\n  </aside>\n\n  <main>\n    <div class=\"doc-head\">\n      <div class=\"breadcrumbs\">{{breadcrumb}}</div>\n      <h1>{{title}}</h1>\n      <div class=\"pill-row\">{{header-pills}}</div>\n      <dl class=\"meta-grid\">{{meta-grid}}</dl>\n    </div>\n\n    {{body-html}}\n  </main>\n\n  <footer>Rendered from <code>{{source-path}}</code> on {{render-date}} · regenerate to update.</footer>\n</div>\n\n<script>\n  // Scrollspy: highlight the TOC entry for the section currently in view.\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('aside nav a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n```\n\n## Design notes\n\n- **Inter via system fallback** — the font stack starts with `\"Inter\"` for environments where it's installed; otherwise falls back to `ui-sans-serif` / `system-ui`. The `font-feature-settings` lines (`cv11`, `ss01`, `ss03`) activate Inter's curly-l, single-storey-a, and rounded variants when Inter is present; they're harmless no-ops otherwise. **No web-font load** — that would violate the hard rule.\n- **Indigo accent** — `#5b4cff` (light) / `#8b7cff` (dark). Used for active nav, links, decision callouts (via P3), and primary card borders on hover. Soft variant `--accent-soft` for fills.\n- **Semantic colour tokens** — `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, each with a soft companion for low-contrast fills. Pills, callouts, and chips draw from this palette consistently; pattern authors should not introduce ad-hoc hex values.\n- **Sidebar is the primary nav** — 260px sticky column, numbered entries (`01`, `02`, …), scrollspy-highlighted. Brand block at the top is the doc family (\"Nestl · ADRs\", \"PDE · Roadmap\"). Status block at the bottom is the one-line state line.\n- **Doc head is structured** — breadcrumbs, H1, pill row, metadata grid. Even minimal docs show source + render date in the metadata grid, so provenance is always visible without footer-hunting.\n- **Section headers carry index + h2** — every body section wraps in `<section class=\"s\"><header><span class=\"ix\">01</span><h2>…</h2></header>…</section>`. Patterns that attach colour tags (P3) append a `.tag` span inside the `<h2>`.\n- **Prose defaults are styled** — even a section that matches no pattern still gets readable type, table styling, blockquote, pre/code treatment. The fallback is \"well-typeset doc,\" not \"raw HTML.\"\n- **Print** — sidebar hidden, main expands. Reviewers print-to-PDF for offline annotation get usable output.\n- **Responsive** — below 880px the sidebar collapses above the body and nav becomes a horizontal pill list. Skill is desktop-first but mobile shouldn't be broken.\n- **Dark mode** — `prefers-color-scheme: dark` swaps every variable. No toggle UI (out of scope).\n\n## When pattern treatments arrive\n\nPattern markup substitutes into `{{body-html}}` — the shell itself does not change. Pattern CSS appends to the inline `<style>` block via additions, not replacements. Every pattern class is prefixed `p-`; the shell uses no `p-` classes, so collisions are impossible. The fallback (well-typeset prose with index + h2) is the default for any section the catalogue doesn't classify.\n"
}

  "content": "---\nname: html-patterns\ndescription: >\n  Pattern catalogue for the render-html skill. Each entry maps a recognisable\n  markdown shape to a product-UI HTML treatment — section-header tags, hover\n  cards, decision panels, kanban columns, chips, metadata grids. The skill\n  consults this file during conversion (Step 4) and substitutes the matching\n  markup into {{body-html}}.\ntype: reference\ncited_by:\n  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md\n---\n\n# HTML pattern catalogue\n\nThe render-html skill walks the markdown body section by section. For each section, it checks the patterns below in order. The **first match wins** — if no pattern matches, the section renders with the shell's prose defaults (which are themselves styled — see `html-skeleton.md`).\n\nEvery pattern is defined by:\n\n1. **Detect** — the markdown signal that triggers the treatment. Cheap to check; conservative (false-negative is fine, false-positive is bad — wrongly-treated content is harder to debug than untreated).\n2. **HTML** — the output markup. All class names are prefixed `p-` so they don't collide with the shell's utilities.\n3. **CSS** — additions appended to the shell's inline `<style>` block. The shell's prose styles remain the fallback; pattern CSS only adds, never overrides.\n\nThe hard self-containment rule from `html-skeleton.md` applies to every pattern: no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. Inline SVG is preferred for visual elements. Colour tokens come from the shell's `:root` palette (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, each with a `-soft` variant) — patterns must not introduce ad-hoc hex values.\n\n---\n\n## P1 — Alternatives considered → side-by-side cards\n\n**Detect.** A section whose heading text matches `/alternatives?( considered)?/i`, OR a section containing two-to-four sibling H3s each followed by a bulleted list whose first item starts with `Pros:` or `Cons:` (case-insensitive). Maximum four columns — beyond that, fall back to prose (columns become unreadable). **Does not match** ADR `Rationale` sections whose options use bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**`) rather than H3 — those go to P11.\n\n**HTML.** Each alternative becomes a hover-state card in an auto-fit grid; pros/cons/cost/reversibility render as a small definition list inside.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-alt-grid\">\n  <article class=\"p-alt-card\">\n    <header><h3>Alternative A — short name</h3><span class=\"p-alt-verdict p-alt-verdict-chosen\">Chosen</span></header>\n    <dl>\n      <dt>Pros</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>\n      <dt>Cons</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>\n      <dt>Cost</dt><dd>…</dd>\n      <dt>Reversibility</dt><dd>…</dd>\n    </dl>\n  </article>\n  <!-- repeat per alternative -->\n</div>\n```\n\nThe verdict pill (`Chosen` / `Rejected` / em-dash) is optional — apply only if the source explicitly marks one option as chosen.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-alt-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr)); gap: 14px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-alt-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; transition: border-color 0.15s, transform 0.15s; }\n.p-alt-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }\n.p-alt-card > header { display: flex; justify-content: space-between; align-items: baseline; gap: 8px; margin-bottom: 10px; }\n.p-alt-card h3 { margin: 0; font-size: 14.5px; font-weight: 650; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-alt-card dl { margin: 0; }\n.p-alt-card dt { font-size: 10.5px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.06em; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 10px; font-weight: 600; }\n.p-alt-card dd { margin: 4px 0 0; font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n.p-alt-card ul { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; }\n.p-alt-verdict { font: 600 10.5px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; padding: 3px 7px; border-radius: 4px; }\n.p-alt-verdict-chosen { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); }\n.p-alt-verdict-rejected { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--muted); }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Each card is an `<article>` with a unique `<h3>`; cards appear in DOM order so screen readers read them sequentially. The verdict pill text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.\n\n---\n\n## P2 — Task list with dependencies → vertical timeline\n\n**Detect.** A section containing an ordered list (or sequence of H3s named `Task N — title` / `Step N`) where two or more entries mention \"depends on\", \"after\", \"blocks\", or \"blocked by\" in their first paragraph. Pure task lists without dependency language stay as ordinary ordered lists.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ol class=\"p-timeline\">\n  <li class=\"p-task\" id=\"task-1\">\n    <div class=\"p-task-marker\" aria-hidden=\"true\">1</div>\n    <div class=\"p-task-body\">\n      <h3>Task 1 — name</h3>\n      <p class=\"p-task-deps\">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>\n      <p>Task description prose…</p>\n    </div>\n  </li>\n  <!-- repeat per task; dependency lines link to #task-N -->\n</ol>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 20px 0; position: relative; }\n.p-timeline::before { content: \"\"; position: absolute; left: 15px; top: 4px; bottom: 4px; width: 2px; background: var(--rule); }\n.p-task { position: relative; padding-left: 48px; margin-bottom: 20px; }\n.p-task-marker { position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; width: 32px; height: 32px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--surface); border: 2px solid var(--accent); color: var(--accent); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; font-weight: 650; font-size: 13px; font-family: ui-monospace, monospace; }\n.p-task-body { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 14px 16px; }\n.p-task-body h3 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 14.5px; font-weight: 650; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 12.5px; margin: 0 0 8px; }\n.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }\n.p-task-deps a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href=\"#task-2\">Task 2</a>`); marker is `aria-hidden`.\n\n---\n\n## P3 — Design-doc blocks → tagged H2 + accent panel\n\n**Detect.** H2 headings whose text matches one of: `Problem`, `Context`, `Constraints`, `Decision` (or `Recommended approach`), `Consequences`, `Open questions`. Apply per matching H2 — sibling non-matching H2s render with the shell's plain section header.\n\n**HTML.** The H2 picks up a small tag pill matching the section type. For `Decision`, the body wraps in a **decision panel** (gradient-filled, indigo border, with a `→ We will` label). Other matches use a thin coloured left-border on the section body but otherwise inherit prose defaults — the type-tag does the visual work.\n\n```html\n<section class=\"s p-block p-block-decision\" id=\"decision\">\n  <header>\n    <span class=\"ix\">02</span>\n    <h2>Decision <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-decision\">Chosen</span></h2>\n  </header>\n  <div class=\"p-decision-panel\">\n    <span class=\"p-decision-label\">→ We will</span>\n    Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.\n  </div>\n</section>\n```\n\n```html\n<section class=\"s p-block p-block-context\" id=\"context\">\n  <header>\n    <span class=\"ix\">01</span>\n    <h2>Context <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-context\">Background</span></h2>\n  </header>\n  <p>…</p>\n</section>\n```\n\nTag-label mapping: `Problem` → `Problem` (danger), `Context` → `Background` (muted), `Constraints` → `Limits` (warn), `Decision` → `Chosen` (info / accent), `Consequences` → `Outcomes` (ok), `Open questions` → `Open` (accent-soft).\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-block-tag { margin-left: 10px; font: 600 10.5px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; vertical-align: 2px; padding: 3px 7px; border-radius: 4px; }\n.p-block-tag-problem    { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }\n.p-block-tag-context    { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }\n.p-block-tag-constraints{ background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }\n.p-block-tag-decision   { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }\n.p-block-tag-consequences{ background: var(--ok-soft);    color: var(--ok); }\n.p-block-tag-open-questions { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }\n\n.p-decision-panel {\n  background: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--info-soft), transparent 80%);\n  border: 1px solid var(--info); border-radius: 12px;\n  padding: 20px 22px; margin: 12px 0;\n  font-size: 16px; line-height: 1.5; color: var(--fg); font-weight: 500;\n}\n.p-decision-panel .p-decision-label { display: block; font: 600 11px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.1em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--info); margin-bottom: 8px; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the tag text carries the meaning. The decision panel's label (`→ We will`) is a real text node, not a pseudo-element, so screen readers announce it.\n\n**Sub-buckets inside `Consequences`.** When a `Consequences` section's body contains H3s (or bold-paragraph headings) named `Positive`, `Negative`, `Neutral` / `Open`, render each as a coloured sub-card rather than as nested H3s:\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-conseq-grid\">\n  <div class=\"p-conseq p-conseq-positive\"><h4>Positive</h4><ul>…</ul></div>\n  <div class=\"p-conseq p-conseq-negative\"><h4>Negative</h4><ul>…</ul></div>\n  <div class=\"p-conseq p-conseq-neutral\"><h4>Neutral</h4><ul>…</ul></div>\n</div>\n```\n\n```css\n.p-conseq-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(220px, 1fr)); gap: 12px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-conseq { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left-width: 4px; border-radius: 8px; padding: 12px 14px; }\n.p-conseq h4 { margin: 0 0 8px; font: 600 11px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; }\n.p-conseq-positive { border-left-color: var(--ok);     } .p-conseq-positive h4 { color: var(--ok); }\n.p-conseq-negative { border-left-color: var(--danger); } .p-conseq-negative h4 { color: var(--danger); }\n.p-conseq-neutral  { border-left-color: var(--muted);  } .p-conseq-neutral h4  { color: var(--muted); }\n```\n\nThis is a P3 extension, not a separate pattern — the parent section is still `p-block-consequences`.\n\n**Consequences as checklist (when flat).** If the `Consequences` body is a single flat bullet list with no sub-buckets, render each bullet as a checklist-card row:\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-conseq-list\">\n  <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</span></div>\n</div>\n```\n\n```css\n.p-conseq-list { display: grid; gap: 8px; margin: 12px 0; }\n.p-conseq-item { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 28px 1fr; gap: 12px; align-items: start; padding: 12px 14px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-conseq-check { width: 22px; height: 22px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: grid; place-items: center; font-size: 13px; font-weight: 700; }\n```\n\n---\n\n## P4 — NFRs table → highlighted numeric table\n\n**Detect.** Markdown table inside a section whose heading matches `/non-functional|NFRs?/i`, OR any table whose header row contains one of: `Target`, `SLO`, `p95`, `p99`, `Latency`, `Throughput`, `Availability`, `Budget`.\n\n**HTML.** Standard `<table>` with `class=\"p-nfr\"`; cells that parse as numbers (optionally with units `ms`, `s`, `%`, `/s`, `KB`, `MB`) get `class=\"p-num\"`.\n\n```html\n<table class=\"p-nfr\">\n  <thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>Target</th><th>Current</th></tr></thead>\n  <tbody>\n    <tr><td>p95 latency</td><td class=\"p-num\">200 ms</td><td class=\"p-num\">340 ms</td></tr>\n  </tbody>\n</table>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-nfr td.p-num { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; font-size: 13px; }\n.p-nfr tbody tr:hover { background: var(--rule-soft); }\n```\n\nThe shell already styles `<table>` cleanly; P4 only adds numeric-cell alignment + hover. **Accessibility.** Standard table semantics; `<thead>`/`<tbody>` preserved.\n\n---\n\n## P5 — Open questions → checklist with owner / due\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?/i`. Body is a bullet list; each bullet may end with parenthetical metadata `(owner: name, due: 2026-06-01)` or markdown task-list syntax `- [ ]` / `- [x]`.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ul class=\"p-questions\">\n  <li class=\"p-q\">\n    <input type=\"checkbox\" disabled>\n    <span class=\"p-q-text\">Should we use Postgres or DynamoDB for the audit log?</span>\n    <span class=\"p-q-meta\"><span class=\"pill pill-meta\">@anton</span><span class=\"pill pill-meta\">due 2026-06-01</span></span>\n  </li>\n</ul>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-questions { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-q { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto; gap: 12px; align-items: center; padding: 10px 14px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; margin-bottom: 6px; }\n.p-q input[type=checkbox] { accent-color: var(--accent); }\n.p-q-text { color: var(--fg); font-size: 13.5px; }\n.p-q-meta { display: flex; gap: 6px; }\n```\n\nOwner / due metadata reuses the shell's `.pill .pill-meta` utility. **Accessibility.** Checkbox is `disabled` (read-only display); metadata is visible text, not buried in `title=`.\n\n---\n\n## P6 — Diff / before-after → two-column annotated diff\n\n**Detect.** Two adjacent fenced code blocks within the same H3 / paragraph where the first is labelled (in info string or preceding line) `before`, `was`, `old`, `current`, and the second `after`, `now`, `new`, `proposed`. Also: any code block with info string `diff` renders inline with `+`/`-` line colouring.\n\n**HTML (two-column).**\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-diff\">\n  <div class=\"p-diff-side p-diff-before\">\n    <header>Before</header>\n    <pre><code>…</code></pre>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-diff-side p-diff-after\">\n    <header>After</header>\n    <pre><code>…</code></pre>\n  </div>\n</div>\n```\n\n**HTML (inline diff).** Standard `<pre><code class=\"language-diff\">` with each line wrapped: `<span class=\"p-diff-add\">+ …</span>`, `<span class=\"p-diff-del\">- …</span>`.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-diff { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 12px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-diff-side header { font: 600 11px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 6px; }\n.p-diff-before pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--danger); }\n.p-diff-after  pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--ok); }\n.p-diff-add { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: block; }\n.p-diff-del { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); display: block; }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-diff { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Headers (`Before` / `After`) carry the meaning; colour stripe is supplemental.\n\n---\n\n## P7 — Roadmap Now / Next / Later → three-column kanban\n\n**Detect.** Three sibling H2s or H3s with text exactly matching (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant): `Now`, `Next`, `Later`, **AND** each of those sections' body is predominantly a single `<ul>`/`<ol>` (no nested H3s, no Problem/Appetite/ICE callout sub-structure). If only two of the three columns are present and the bullet-only condition holds, still render as a kanban with the missing column empty. If the bullet-only condition fails (sections contain rich prose, callouts, or H3 sub-items per column), **do not apply** — fall back to prose so the column content stays readable.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-kanban\">\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"now\">\n    <header><span class=\"p-kanban-dot\"></span>Now</header>\n    <ul>\n      <li>Item one</li>\n      <li>Item two</li>\n    </ul>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"next\"> … </div>\n  <div class=\"p-kanban-col\" data-col=\"later\"> … </div>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-kanban { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 12px; margin: 20px 0; }\n.p-kanban-col { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 14px 16px; min-height: 140px; }\n.p-kanban-col header { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; font: 600 11px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; padding-bottom: 10px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); margin-bottom: 10px; }\n.p-kanban-dot { width: 8px; height: 8px; border-radius: 50%; }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"now\"]   header { color: var(--ok); }     .p-kanban-col[data-col=\"now\"]   .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--ok); }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"next\"]  header { color: var(--info); }   .p-kanban-col[data-col=\"next\"]  .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--info); }\n.p-kanban-col[data-col=\"later\"] header { color: var(--muted); }  .p-kanban-col[data-col=\"later\"] .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--muted); }\n.p-kanban-col ul { padding-left: 18px; margin: 0; font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n.p-kanban-col li { margin: 6px 0; }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-kanban { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Each column is keyboard-navigable as ordinary text; the dot + header colour are supplemental to the label.\n\n---\n\n## P8 — ICE scores → coloured score chips\n\n**Detect.** Markdown table whose header row contains EITHER (a) `Impact`, `Confidence`, and `Ease` (in any order, plus an optional `Score` column), OR (b) a single `ICE` column alongside at least one of `Confidence` / `Slot` / `Theme` / `Kano`. Numeric cells are graded on the same red/amber/green band regardless of column. Em-dash placeholders (`—`) render as a muted chip.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<table class=\"p-ice\">\n  <thead><tr><th>Idea</th><th>Impact</th><th>Confidence</th><th>Ease</th><th>ICE</th></tr></thead>\n  <tbody>\n    <tr>\n      <td>Onboarding revamp</td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"green\">9</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">5</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"red\">3</span></td>\n      <td><span class=\"p-chip\" data-band=\"amber\">135</span></td>\n    </tr>\n  </tbody>\n</table>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-ice .p-chip { display: inline-block; min-width: 30px; padding: 3px 9px; border-radius: 999px; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 12px; text-align: center; font-weight: 650; }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"red\"]   { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"amber\"] { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"green\"] { background: var(--ok-soft);     color: var(--ok); }\n.p-chip[data-band=\"muted\"] { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }\n.p-ice td:not(:first-child) { text-align: center; }\n```\n\nChip bands: `1–3 → red`, `4–6 → amber`, `7–10 → green`, em-dash / missing → `muted`. ICE totals (any value above 10) are graded against the same 50 / 100 / 200 thresholds used for sequencing in roadmaps that follow `PRODUCT_RULES`. **Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; numeric value is the source of truth.\n\n---\n\n## P9 — YAML frontmatter → metadata grid (lifted into doc head)\n\n**Detect.** Source markdown begins with a YAML frontmatter block delimited by `---` on its own line, before the H1. Common in ADRs and design docs (fields like `id`, `status`, `date`, `authors`, `linear`, `supersedes`).\n\n**HTML.** Frontmatter fields **lift into the shell's `{{meta-grid}}` slot** under the doc head — they do not render as a separate block in the body. The shell's `.meta-grid` styles already cover the visual treatment; P9 only specifies the *lifting* behaviour. The `status` field also lifts into `{{header-pills}}` via the P10 pill treatment.\n\n```html\n<!-- inside the shell's <dl class=\"meta-grid\"> -->\n<dt>id</dt>      <dd>ADR-engagement-modifier-constants</dd>\n<dt>authors</dt> <dd>Anton Babushkin</dd>\n<dt>linear</dt>  <dd><a href=\"https://linear.app/…\">ABA-110</a></dd>\n<dt>supersedes</dt><dd>—</dd>\n<dt>source</dt>  <dd>docs/adrs/engagement-modifier-constants.md</dd>\n<dt>rendered</dt><dd>2026-05-17</dd>\n```\n\n`source` and `rendered` are always included; frontmatter fields fold in alphabetically after them, except `status` (which becomes a header pill, not a grid row) and `date` (which becomes a date pill in the header). **Do not drop frontmatter on the floor** — readers use it to anchor the doc.\n\n**Accessibility.** Definition-list semantics; screen readers announce each key-value pair.\n\n---\n\n## P10 — Status / state values → coloured pill\n\n**Detect.** A frontmatter `status:` field, OR a top-of-doc bold line matching `/\\*\\*Status:\\*\\*\\s+(\\w+)/i`, OR a single-cell `Status` column in a table. Recognised values (case-insensitive): `accepted`, `proposed`, `draft`, `rejected`, `superseded`, `deprecated`, `flagged`, `ok`.\n\n**HTML.** When detected from frontmatter or top-of-doc bold line, the pill **lifts into the shell's `{{header-pills}}` slot** (using the shell's `.pill` utility — no new CSS needed):\n\n```html\n<span class=\"pill pill-accepted\"><span class=\"dot\"></span>Accepted</span>\n```\n\nWhen detected inside a table cell, render in-place:\n\n```html\n<td><span class=\"pill pill-accepted\"><span class=\"dot\"></span>Accepted</span></td>\n```\n\nThe shell already defines `.pill-accepted`, `.pill-proposed`, `.pill-rejected`, `.pill-superseded`, etc. P10 adds no new CSS — it specifies the *detection + placement* contract. **Accessibility.** Label text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.\n\n---\n\n## P11 — Rationale \"Why X\" blocks → hover-state cards\n\n**Detect.** A section heading matches `/rationale/i` AND its body contains two-or-more bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**` / `**Why not Y:**`) each followed by a bullet list. Common in ADRs that consider multiple alternatives without dedicated H3s per option.\n\n**HTML.** Each bold paragraph becomes a card heading; the following list is the card body. Cards stack in a single-column grid (one per row) so each option gets full line-length for its bullets, but pick up an indigo border on hover and a directional chevron in the title — `✓` for `Why X`, `✗` for `Why not Y`.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-why-grid\">\n  <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-pro\">\n    <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✓</span>Why Rails</h3>\n    <ul><li>…</li></ul>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-con\">\n    <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✗</span>Why not a separate frontend</h3>\n    <ul><li>…</li></ul>\n  </div>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-why-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr; gap: 12px; margin: 16px 0; }\n.p-why-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; transition: border-color 0.15s; }\n.p-why-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }\n.p-why-card h3 { margin: 0 0 12px; font-size: 14px; font-weight: 650; display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-why-chev { font-size: 16px; line-height: 1; }\n.p-why-pro .p-why-chev { color: var(--ok); }\n.p-why-con .p-why-chev { color: var(--warn); }\n.p-why-card ul { margin: 0; padding: 0; list-style: none; }\n.p-why-card li { position: relative; padding: 4px 0 4px 18px; font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }\n.p-why-card li::before { content: \"\"; position: absolute; left: 4px; top: 12px; width: 4px; height: 4px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--muted); }\n```\n\n**Precedence.** P11 ranks below P1 (Alternatives). When both could match (`Rationale` section whose options also have `Pros:`/`Cons:`), P1's side-by-side card grid is the stronger treatment.\n\n**Accessibility.** `<h3>` keeps headings in the document outline; lists keep list semantics; chevron is a real text node (not pseudo-content) so screen readers announce it.\n\n---\n\n## P12 — Labelled rubric cards (C1, C2, FR3 …) → repeated sub-field cards\n\n**Detect.** Two or more sibling H3s whose text matches `/^([A-Z]+\\d+)\\s+[—–-]\\s+/` (e.g. `C1 — Deadband: ±2%`, `FR3 — Idempotent writes`, `OQ5 — …`), AND each section repeats the same set of bold-paragraph sub-fields (e.g. **Reasoning**, **Alternative considered**, **Revision trigger**). The identifier prefix (`C`, `FR`, `OQ`, `NFR`, `ADR`) is rendered as a pill on the card header.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-rubric\" id=\"c1\">\n  <h3><span class=\"p-rubric-id\">C1</span> Deadband: ±2% YoY</h3>\n  <div class=\"p-rubric-formula\">If <code>abs(yoy_change) &lt; 0.02</code>, set <code>direction = 0</code> …</div>\n  <h4>Reasoning</h4><p>…</p>\n  <h4>Alternative considered</h4><p>…</p>\n  <h4>Revision trigger</h4><p>…</p>\n</div>\n```\n\nThe first paragraph (often a one-line formal definition or formula) becomes `p-rubric-formula`. Subsequent bold-paragraph sub-fields become `<h4>` headings inside the card.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-rubric { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 20px; margin: 16px 0; transition: border-color 0.15s; }\n.p-rubric:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }\n.p-rubric h3 { margin: 0 0 8px; display: flex; gap: 10px; align-items: baseline; font-size: 15px; font-weight: 650; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-rubric-id { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 12px; padding: 3px 8px; background: var(--accent); color: white; border-radius: 4px; letter-spacing: 0.02em; }\n.p-rubric h4 { font: 600 11px/1 ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin: 14px 0 4px; }\n.p-rubric-formula { background: var(--accent-soft); border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); padding: 10px 14px; margin: 10px 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 12.5px; border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-rubric p { margin: 0 0 8px; }\n```\n\n**Why a dedicated pattern.** Plain-prose rendering of a 6-card ADR section (C1–C6 with Reasoning / Alternative / Revision trigger each) produces a wall of repeating H3+H4 that the reader has to mentally segment. The card boundary plus the identifier pill makes \"which constant am I reading\" answerable at a glance.\n\n**Accessibility.** Heading levels (`<h3>` outer, `<h4>` inner) preserve the document outline; the card boundary is decorative.\n\n---\n\n## Fallback\n\nA section that matches no pattern renders with the shell's section-header treatment (`<section class=\"s\"><header><span class=\"ix\">NN</span><h2>…</h2></header>`) and the shell's prose defaults (`<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, `<pre>`, `<blockquote>`). The fallback is \"well-typeset doc,\" not raw HTML — false-positive pattern matches degrade review trust faster than missing treatments.\n\n## Adding a new pattern\n\nWhen a new pattern earns its slot:\n\n1. Add a section here following the same six-part structure: heading, **Detect**, **HTML**, **CSS**, **Accessibility**, brief rationale.\n2. Number it sequentially (`P13 — …`) so cross-references stay stable.\n3. Confirm the CSS uses shell colour tokens (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, `-soft` variants) — no ad-hoc hex values.\n4. Confirm the CSS only adds new selectors prefixed `p-` — it must not override any rule in `html-skeleton.md`.\n5. If the detection signal could overlap an existing pattern, document the precedence rule explicitly. The first-match-wins ordering of this file is the resolution mechanism.\n"
}
```

1	# Roadmap
2	
3	Last updated: 2026-05-03
4	Cycle: Now / Next / Later (no fixed date horizon — solo personal project)
5	
6	---
7	
8	## Strategic context
9	
10	Phase 1 MVP is largely complete: Funda scraping, bike time + commute enrichment, deal-breaker filtering, weighted scoring, listing feed + detail, shortlist, scoring profile config, admin dashboard.
11	
12	**This cycle's priorities (inferred from PRD phases — confirm before committing):**
13	1. Close the partner-participation gap: the primary user's partner cannot participate in listing evaluation without a shared view. This is the single biggest functional hole relative to the PRD vision.
14	2. Close the "works while you sleep" promise: timed digest email is validated for a build slot; push notifications need a gap audit before committing.
15	3. Validate neighbourhood intelligence as the next differentiation layer before building it.
16	
17	> ⚠️ **Input gap**: strategic priorities are inferred from PRD §2–§6 rather than an explicit cycle goal statement. If priorities have shifted (e.g. active search is paused, or a specific Phase 3 item is time-sensitive), adjust slot assignments before acting on this roadmap.

27	| Differentiators (`diff`) | 35% |
28	| Incrementals (`incr`) | 15% |

45	| Map view + cycling routes | `incr` | — | — | Performance | Later |

54	
55	## Now
56	
57	### Shareable shortlist — partner reactions and notes
58	
59	**Problem:** For a two-person household evaluating Amsterdam rentals remotely, we believe the absence of a shared view into the scored shortlist is causing joint listing decisions to be made via screenshots and messaging apps rather than within the tool, fragmenting the evaluation workflow and preventing the partner from participating with structured reactions or notes.
60	
61	**Success criterion:** Partner accesses the shared shortlist URL and leaves at least one reaction or note on a listing within 72 hours of the first share event.
62	
63	**Appetite:** 2 weeks. Cap, not estimate. If full scope (reactions + notes + combined status) doesn't fit, cut to: share URL → partner read-only view → like/dislike reaction. Notes and combined status move to Next.
64	
65	**Theme:** `diff` — no Dutch rental platform supports partner co-review without accounts.
66	
67	**ICE:** 8 × 5 × 4 = 160
68	
69	**Kano:** Must-be. Without it, the partner cannot participate. Absence blocks the core household workflow.
70	
71	---
72	
73	## Next
74	
75	### Daily digest email — Melbourne morning timing
76	
77	**Problem:** For an expat user in the Melbourne timezone checking Amsterdam listings, we believe the absence of a proactive daily summary delivered at the right local time is causing the app to fail its "works while you sleep" promise: new listings require active app-checking rather than arriving in the user's inbox when they are ready to act.
78	
79	**Theme:** `incr`
80	
81	**ICE:** 6 × 5 × 7 = 210
82	
83	**Note:** Sequence after push notification validation resolves. If push notifications are validated and built, design digest and push together to avoid notification fatigue (a listing shouldn't appear in both a push alert and the same morning's digest). If push is killed, digest ships standalone.
84	
85	---

88	
89	These are strategic hypotheses, not commitments. No scoping until they move to Next.
90	
91	### Map view with listing locations and cycling routes
92	Helps users orient listings spatially. No confirmed gap in current tool — users may not need it if scores are sufficient.
93	
94	### Pararius / HousingAnywhere adapters
95	Expands coverage beyond Funda. Priority rises if Funda coverage proves insufficient or listings become scarce.
96

98	Post-viewing corrections to provisional scores. Low priority until listings are being visited in person.
99	
100	### Proximity scoring (parks, supermarkets, transit via Places API)
101	Enriches the Neighbourhood Vibe section with data. Complements — and possibly supersedes — the Neighbourhood RAG validation bet.

103	---
104	
105	## Validation slots
106	
107	### Push notifications — high-scoring listings
108	
109	**Hypothesis:** Proactive alerts for high-scoring listings would reduce the window between ingestion and user awareness, reducing the risk of missing a listing before acting on it.
110	
111	**Why it's here:** Manual checking habit confirmed in PRD. But the platform already has a near-real-time ingestion path via forwarded email alerts. The user-awareness gap (listing ingested → user notified) may not exist or may already be closed. Problem is plausible but not confirmed.
112	
113	**Validation method:** Gap audit (≤2 hours, no code).
114	1. Review last 2–4 weeks of ingested listings. For each high-scoring listing: when ingested vs. when first viewed. Is there a consistent multi-hour delay?
115	2. Trace the Funda email alert → forwarded → ingestion flow. Does ingestion already produce a visible user-facing signal?
116	3. If gap is confirmed: assess whether a triggered mailer (easy, ~1 day) closes it before committing to web push infrastructure.
117	
118	**Confidence target:** Raise from 2 → 6. If gap audit shows no meaningful delay: kill or defer. If confirmed: move to idea bank and schedule after digest email ships.
119	
120	**Current ICE:** 7 × 2 × 6 = 84. If validated: projected 7 × 6 × 6 = 252.
121	
122	---
123	
124	### Neighbourhood RAG — "About this area" panel
125	
126	**Hypothesis:** An AI-generated neighbourhood brief (free text + concerns + citations) would replace the user's manual Reddit/Google research step per listing, accelerating the shortlist-to-viewing-decision workflow.
127	
128	**Why it's here:** Problem confirmed (manual step exists). Solution unvalidated — Dutch-language source coverage, RAG quality for hyper-local facts, and hallucination risk are all unknown.
129	
130	**Validation method:** 1-day spike.
131	1. Pick 3–5 Amsterdam neighbourhoods (one well-reviewed, one contested, one obscure).
132	2. Fetch top Reddit/Google results manually for each.
133	3. Prompt Claude: generate free-text brief + concerns list + citations from that content.
134	4. Evaluate: Is the output accurate? Does it surface real concerns? Would you trust it over doing the research yourself?
135	
136	**Confidence target:** Raise from 2 → 6. If spike output is not trustworthy: kill or reshape as curated-links fallback (lower impact, no hallucination risk).
137	
138	**Current ICE:** 7 × 2 × 3 = 42. If validated: projected 7 × 7 × 3 = 147.
139	
140	---
141	
142	### Q&A agent — shortlist chat
143	
144	**Hypothesis:** A natural-language chat interface on the shortlist would let users compare listings and ask cross-listing questions faster than reading individual cards.
145	
146	**Why it's here:** Problem unconfirmed. The tool already answers "which listings are worth my time" via scoring and ranking. Whether residual unanswered questions exist after seeing scored cards is unknown.
147	
148	**Validation method:** Self-observation (≤5 real sessions).
149	Log every "I want to know X and the UI can't tell me" moment. Categorise each gap. If no moments: kill. If gaps are confirmed: check whether a sort/filter control solves it cheaper than a chat UI.
150	
151	**Confidence target:** Raise from 1 → 5 minimum before any build work. Natural language requires evidence beyond "comparison need exists."
152	
153	**Current ICE:** 6 × 1 × 2 = 12.
154

The app has a standard web UI (listing feed, detail pages, forms), background job processing (scraping, email ingestion), and no complex client-side interactivity. It will be built and maintained by a solo developer.</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"decision\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">02</span><h2>Decision <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-decision\">Chosen</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-decision-panel\">\n        <span class=\"p-decision-label\">→ We will</span>\n        Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.\n      </div>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"rationale\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">03</span><h2>Rationale <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-rationale\">4 reasons</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-why-grid\">\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-pro\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✓</span>Why Rails</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Full-stack framework: models, views, controllers, jobs, mailers, email ingestion (Action Mailbox) all in one codebase</li>\n            <li>Rails 8 defaults (SQLite, Solid Queue, Solid Cache, Solid Cable, Kamal) eliminate external dependencies — no Redis, no Postgres, no separate job runner</li>\n            <li>Mature scraping ecosystem (Nokogiri is bundled, HTTP.rb for HTTP client)</li>\n            <li>Convention-over-configuration means less decision fatigue for a solo developer</li>\n            <li>The app is fundamentally CRUD + background jobs + server-rendered pages — Rails' sweet spot</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-con\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✗</span>Why not a separate frontend</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>The UI is listing cards, detail pages, and forms — no complex client-side state</li>\n            <li>A separate frontend doubles the deployment surface, adds a build pipeline, and introduces API serialisation overhead</li>\n            <li>Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus) handles the few dynamic interactions needed (shortlist actions, filters) without a JS framework</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-con\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✗</span>Why not Python / FastAPI</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>FastAPI is backend-only; would still need a frontend framework or templating layer</li>\n            <li>Django would be comparable to Rails but the developer prefers Ruby</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-pro\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✓</span>Why SQLite over Postgres</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Single-user app with low write volume (tens of listings per day)</li>\n            <li>Rails 8 has first-class SQLite support including for queues, cache, and WebSockets</li>\n            <li>Zero-ops: no database server to manage</li>\n            <li>Can migrate to Postgres later if multi-user support is added</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n      </div>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"consequences\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">04</span><h2>Consequences <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-consequences\">Outcomes</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-conseq-list\">\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>Background jobs (scraping, email processing, evaluation) run in the same process via Solid Queue</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>Deployment is a single Docker container via Kamal to a VPS</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>If we later need a separate API (e.g. for a native mobile app), we can add API controllers to the same Rails app</span></div>\n      </div>\n    </section>\n  </main>\n\n  <footer>Rendered from <code>docs/adr/001-rails-monolith.md</code> on 2026-05-17 · regenerate to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('aside nav a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
