padding: 6px 0 6px 20px; font-size: 15px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.6; }\n  .p-why-card li::before { content: \"\"; position: absolute; left: 4px; top: 15px; width: 5px; height: 5px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--muted); }\n\n  /* P3 consequences as checklist */\n  .p-conseq-list { display: grid; gap: 10px; margin: 14px 0; }\n  .p-conseq-item { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 32px 1fr; gap: 14px; align-items: start; padding: 14px 16px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.55; color: var(--fg); }\n  .p-conseq-check { width: 24px; height: 24px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: grid; place-items: center; font-size: 14px; font-weight: 700; }\n\n  footer { grid-column: 1 / -1; padding: 24px 64px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 13px; border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n  footer code { color: var(--fg-soft); }\n\n  @media print { aside { display: none; } .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } main { padding: 24px; max-width: none; } }\n  @media (max-width: 880px) { .layout { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } aside { position: static; height: auto; border-right: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); padding: 20px; } main { padding: 32px 24px 60px; } }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<div class=\"layout\">\n  <aside>\n    <div class=\"brand\"><span class=\"dot\"></span><span>Nestl · ADRs</span></div>\n    <p class=\"kicker\">On this page</p>\n    <nav aria-label=\"Table of contents\">\n      <ol>\n        <li><a href=\"#context\">Context</a></li>\n        <li><a href=\"#decision\">Decision</a></li>\n        <li><a href=\"#rationale\">Rationale</a></li>\n        <li><a href=\"#consequences\">Consequences</a></li>\n      </ol>\n    </nav>\n    <p class=\"kicker\">Status</p>\n    <div class=\"status-block\">Accepted 31 Mar 2026.<br>No superseding ADR.</div>\n  </aside>\n\n  <main>\n    <div class=\"doc-head\">\n      <div class=\"breadcrumbs\">Architecture · ADRs · <span>ADR-001</span></div>\n      <h1>Ruby on Rails Monolith</h1>\n      <div class=\"pill-row\">\n        <span class=\"pill pill-accepted\"><span class=\"dot\"></span>Accepted</span>\n        <span class=\"pill pill-meta\">ADR-001</span>\n        <span class=\"pill pill-meta\">31 Mar 2026</span>\n      </div>\n      <dl class=\"meta-grid\">\n        <dt>Source</dt><dd>docs/adr/001-rails-monolith.md</dd>\n        <dt>Rendered</dt><dd>2026-05-17</dd>\n        <dt>Supersedes</dt><dd>—</dd>\n      </dl>\n    </div>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"context\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">01</span><h2>Context <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-context\">Background</span></h2></header>\n      <p>We need to choose a tech stack for Nestl, a single-user rental listing evaluator. The app has a standard web UI (listing feed, detail pages, forms), background job processing (scraping, email ingestion), and no complex client-side interactivity. It will be built and maintained by a solo developer.</p>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"decision\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">02</span><h2>Decision <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-decision\">Chosen</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-decision-panel\">\n        <span class=\"p-decision-label\">→ We will</span>\n        Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.\n      </div>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"rationale\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">03</span><h2>Rationale <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-rationale\">4 reasons</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-why-grid\">\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-pro\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✓</span>Why Rails</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Full-stack framework: models, views, controllers, jobs, mailers, email ingestion (Action Mailbox) all in one codebase</li>\n            <li>Rails 8 defaults (SQLite, Solid Queue, Solid Cache, Solid Cable, Kamal) eliminate external dependencies — no Redis, no Postgres, no separate job runner</li>\n            <li>Mature scraping ecosystem (Nokogiri is bundled, HTTP.rb for HTTP client)</li>\n            <li>Convention-over-configuration means less decision fatigue for a solo developer</li>\n            <li>The app is fundamentally CRUD + background jobs + server-rendered pages — Rails' sweet spot</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-con\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✗</span>Why not a separate frontend</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>The UI is listing cards, detail pages, and forms — no complex client-side state</li>\n            <li>A separate frontend doubles the deployment surface, adds a build pipeline, and introduces API serialisation overhead</li>\n            <li>Hotwire (Turbo + Stimulus) handles the few dynamic interactions needed (shortlist actions, filters) without a JS framework</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-con\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✗</span>Why not Python / FastAPI</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>FastAPI is backend-only; would still need a frontend framework or templating layer</li>\n            <li>Django would be comparable to Rails but the developer prefers Ruby</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n        <div class=\"p-why-card p-why-pro\">\n          <h3><span class=\"p-why-chev\">✓</span>Why SQLite over Postgres</h3>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Single-user app with low write volume (tens of listings per day)</li>\n            <li>Rails 8 has first-class SQLite support including for queues, cache, and WebSockets</li>\n            <li>Zero-ops: no database server to manage</li>\n            <li>Can migrate to Postgres later if multi-user support is added</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n\n      </div>\n    </section>\n\n    <section class=\"s\" id=\"consequences\">\n      <header><span class=\"ix\">04</span><h2>Consequences <span class=\"p-block-tag p-block-tag-consequences\">Outcomes</span></h2></header>\n      <div class=\"p-conseq-list\">\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>Background jobs (scraping, email processing, evaluation) run in the same process via Solid Queue</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>Deployment is a single Docker container via Kamal to a VPS</span></div>\n        <div class=\"p-conseq-item\"><span class=\"p-conseq-check\">→</span><span>If we later need a separate API (e.g. for a native mobile app), we can add API controllers to the same Rails app</span></div>\n      </div>\n    </section>\n  </main>\n\n  <footer>Rendered from <code>docs/adr/001-rails-monolith.md</code> on 2026-05-17 · regenerate to update.</footer>\n</div>\n<script>\n  const links = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('aside nav a'));\n  const targets = links.map(a => document.getElementById(a.getAttribute('href').slice(1))).filter(Boolean);\n  const byId = new Map(targets.map((el, i) => [el.id, links[i]]));\n  const io = new IntersectionObserver(entries => {\n    for (const e of entries) {\n      if (e.isIntersecting) {\n        links.forEach(a => a.classList.remove('active'));\n        byId.get(e.target.id)?.classList.add('active');\n      }\n    }\n  }, { rootMargin: '-30% 0px -60% 0px' });\n  targets.forEach(t => io.observe(t));\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"
}

# Structure & Visual Design of the Implementation Plan

## Document Hierarchy

The page uses a numbered section system (01–06) paired with descriptive headings, creating scannable chapters:

- **Effort callout** (top): Single-line summaries (effort estimate, packages affected, schema changes, feature flag)
- **Six main sections**: Milestones, Data flow, Mockups, Key code, Risks & mitigations, Open questions

## Visual Treatments

**Timeline Layout (Milestones)**
Organizes work into a two-week sprint with phase markers ("Week 1 · Mon–Tue") followed by deliverables. Each phase lists touched packages and migration numbers inline, creating a horizontal narrative of dependencies.

**Data Flow Diagram**
Uses a two-column metaphor: "Optimistic write path on the left, fan-out on the right." Components (React Query, tRPC, Postgres) flow left-to-right with labeled arrows. Line styles differentiate logic: solid lines represent "request/response paths" while "dashed" lines indicate realtime fan-out, with a note that "the composer never waits."

**Mockups Section**
Two wireframe examples (labeled A, B) show the feature in context—a threaded comment card and a sidebar digest—with minimal detail to drive consensus on "nesting depth, composer placement."

**Code Blocks**
Two SQL migration and TypeScript hook examples demonstrate the "two pieces most likely to be done wrong," explicitly calling out soft deletes, read cursors, and temp-id reconciliation logic.

**Risk Table**
Four-column grid: Risk description, Severity badge (HIGH/MED/LOW), Mitigation strategy, and decision owner. Compact and scannable.

## Typography & Navigation

- **Numbered sections** act as anchors for cross-reference ("decide with · design, before slice 2")
- **Inline metadata** (packages, table names, dates) uses monospaced treatment
- **Open questions** cite dependent teams and decision deadlines, deferring choices explicitly

This design prioritizes phone-readability through short paragraphs, chunked information, and visual hierarchy rather than dense narrative.

597	    <section>
598	      <div class="sec-head"><span class="num">04</span><h2>Key code</h2></div>
599	      <p class="sec-intro">The two pieces most likely to be done wrong: the migration (soft deletes, read cursors) and the optimistic mutation (temp-id reconciliation).</p>
600	
601	      <div class="code-grid">
602	        <div class="code-block">
603	          <div class="file-label">packages/db/migrations/0042_comments.sql</div>
604	          <div class="code"><pre><span class="kw">create table</span> <span class="fn">comments</span> (
605	  id          uuid <span class="kw">primary key default</span> gen_random_uuid(),
606	  task_id     uuid <span class="kw">not null references</span> tasks(id),
607	  parent_id   uuid <span class="kw">references</span> comments(id),   <span class="cm">-- one level only,</span>
608	                                                <span class="cm">-- enforced in API</span>
609	  author_id   uuid <span class="kw">not null references</span> users(id),
610	  body        text <span class="kw">not null</span>,
611	  created_at  timestamptz <span class="kw">not null default</span> now(),
612	  deleted_at  timestamptz                       <span class="cm">-- soft delete</span>
613	);
614	
615	<span class="kw">create table</span> <span class="fn">comment_reads</span> (
616	  task_id    uuid <span class="kw">not null references</span> tasks(id),
617	  user_id    uuid <span class="kw">not null references</span> users(id),
618	  read_up_to timestamptz <span class="kw">not null</span>,
619	  <span class="kw">primary key</span> (task_id, user_id)
620	);
621	
622	<span class="kw">create index</span> <span class="fn">comments_task_created</span>
623	  <span class="kw">on</span> comments (task_id, created_at);</pre></div>
624	        </div>
625	
626	        <div class="code-block">
627	          <div class="file-label">apps/web/hooks/useAddComment.ts</div>
628	          <div class="code"><pre><span class="kw">export function</span> <span class="fn">useAddComment</span>(taskId: string) {
629	  <span class="kw">const</span> qc = useQueryClient();
630	  <span class="kw">return</span> trpc.comments.create.<span class="fn">useMutation</span>({
631	    <span class="fn">onMutate</span>: <span class="kw">async</span> (input) =&gt; {
632	      <span class="kw">const</span> temp = { ...input, id: <span class="str">`temp-${nanoid()}`</span>,
633	                     createdAt: <span class="kw">new</span> Date(), pending: <span class="kw">true</span> };
634	      qc.<span class="fn">setQueryData</span>(key(taskId), (prev) =&gt;
635	        [...(prev ?? []), temp]);
636	      <span class="kw">return</span> { tempId: temp.id };
637	    },
638	    <span class="fn">onSuccess</span>: (row, _v, ctx) =&gt; {
639	      <span class="cm">// reconcile temp id → real id so the</span>
640	      <span class="cm">// realtime append doesn't duplicate it</span>
641	      qc.<span class="fn">setQueryData</span>(key(taskId), (prev) =&gt;
642	        prev.map((c) =&gt; c.id === ctx.tempId ? row : c));
643	    },
644	    <span class="fn">onError</span>: (_e, _v, ctx) =&gt; {
645	      qc.<span class="fn">setQueryData</span>(key(taskId), (prev) =&gt;
646	        prev.filter((c) =&gt; c.id !== ctx.tempId));
647	    },
648	  });
649	}</pre></div>
650	        </div>
651	      </div>
652	    </section>
653	
654	    <!-- ============================================================= -->
655	
656	    <section>
657	      <div class="sec-head"><span class="num">05</span><h2>Risks &amp; mitigations</h2></div>
658	      <div class="risks">
659	        <div class="row">
660	          <div class="cell head">Risk</div>
661	          <div class="cell head">Sev</div>
662	          <div class="cell head">Mitigation</div>
663	        </div>
664	        <div class="row">
665	          <div class="cell">Realtime duplicate: socket append races with the HTTP response and the temp-id reconcile.</div>
666	          <div class="cell"><span class="sev high">HIGH</span></div>
667	          <div class="cell">Dedupe on server-assigned <code>id</code> in the cache updater; socket payload carries the real id, temp rows are filtered on reconcile.</div>
668	        </div>
669	        <div class="row">
670	          <div class="cell">Unread counts go stale when a user reads the thread on another device.</div>
671	          <div class="cell"><span class="sev med">MED</span></div>
672	          <div class="cell">Broadcast <code>comment_reads</code> upserts on the same channel; client treats its own cursor as max(local, remote).</div>
673	        </div>
674	        <div class="row">
675	          <div class="cell">Mention detection false-positives on pasted markdown (<code>@media</code>, <code>@2x</code>).</div>
676	          <div class="cell"><span class="sev low">LOW</span></div>
677	          <div class="cell">Resolve mentions against workspace members only, at write time, and store the resolved user ids — never re-parse on read.</div>
678	        </div>
679	      </div>
680	    </section>

690	          <div class="owner">Decide with · design, before slice 2</div>

694	          <div class="qd">Immediate-per-mention will be noisy. Proposal: batch on a 15-minute window, collapse to one email per task, and respect quiet hours from the existing settings table.</div>
695	          <div class="owner">Decide with · platform, before slice 4</div>

```

> AGENT

Looked at Thariq's plan source. The treatments that make it click — and that our current catalogue is missing or weak on:

1. **Summary strip** (top KPI cells: Effort / Packages / Tables / Flag) — anchors the whole doc
2. **SVG data-flow diagram** with a solid/dashed arrow convention + caption — agent-authored inline SVG, not a library
3. **Mockup frames** (labeled `A · ...` with chrome around an inline UI sketch) — for design docs especially
4. **Polished code panel** (dark, file-label header, syntax-token spans) — we have diff but not a clean code block
5. **Risk table** with severity chips (HIGH/MED/LOW) in a three-column grid — we have NFR table but not this shape

Existing patterns that need a polish pass to match: timeline (we have one but the dot-and-line column is weaker), open questions (Thariq's left-accent card is cleaner), alternatives (still fine).

**My recommendation for next:**

1. **Narrow the skill's scope explicitly to plan + design-doc.** Drop the roadmap/idea-bank patterns (P7 kanban, P8 ICE) from the v1 pattern catalogue — they belong in a sibling `render-roadmap` later. Fewer patterns done well beats twelve done okay.
2. **Add the five missing patterns above** to `html-patterns.md`, modeled on Thariq's source (which I now have at `/tmp/thariq-plan.html`).
3. **Render two real artefacts end-to-end as test cases** — one plan, one design doc from `~/src/nestl` or `~/src/stock-review` — to validate the pattern set before writing the SKILL.md.
4. Once both look right, write `skills/render-html/SKILL.md` against the now-stable references.

Want me to proceed in that order? If yes, I'll start with step 1 (trimming patterns) + step 2 (adding the five), then come back with two rendered test artefacts for you to eyeball before we touch the SKILL.md.

```
1	---
2	name: html-patterns
3	description: >
4	  Pattern catalogue for the render-html skill. Each entry maps a recognisable
5	  markdown shape to a product-UI HTML treatment — section-header tags, hover
6	  cards, decision panels, kanban columns, chips, metadata grids. The skill
7	  consults this file during conversion (Step 4) and substitutes the matching
8	  markup into {{body-html}}.
9	type: reference
10	cited_by:
11	  - skills/render-html/SKILL.md
12	---
13	
14	# HTML pattern catalogue
15	
16	The render-html skill walks the markdown body section by section. For each section, it checks the patterns below in order. The **first match wins** — if no pattern matches, the section renders with the shell's prose defaults (which are themselves styled — see `html-skeleton.md`).
17	
18	Every pattern is defined by:
19	
20	1. **Detect** — the markdown signal that triggers the treatment. Cheap to check; conservative (false-negative is fine, false-positive is bad — wrongly-treated content is harder to debug than untreated).
21	2. **HTML** — the output markup. All class names are prefixed `p-` so they don't collide with the shell's utilities.
22	3. **CSS** — additions appended to the shell's inline `<style>` block. The shell's prose styles remain the fallback; pattern CSS only adds, never overrides.
23	
24	The hard self-containment rule from `html-skeleton.md` applies to every pattern: no external CSS, no external JS, no remote images. Inline SVG is preferred for visual elements. Colour tokens come from the shell's `:root` palette (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, each with a `-soft` variant) — patterns must not introduce ad-hoc hex values.
25	
26	---
27	
28	## P1 — Alternatives considered → side-by-side cards
29	
30	**Detect.** A section whose heading text matches `/alternatives?( considered)?/i`, OR a section containing two-to-four sibling H3s each followed by a bulleted list whose first item starts with `Pros:` or `Cons:` (case-insensitive). Maximum four columns — beyond that, fall back to prose (columns become unreadable). **Does not match** ADR `Rationale` sections whose options use bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**`) rather than H3 — those go to P11.
31	
32	**HTML.** Each alternative becomes a hover-state card in an auto-fit grid; pros/cons/cost/reversibility render as a small definition list inside.
33	
34	```html
35	<div class="p-alt-grid">
36	  <article class="p-alt-card">
37	    <header><h3>Alternative A — short name</h3><span class="p-alt-verdict p-alt-verdict-chosen">Chosen</span></header>
38	    <dl>
39	      <dt>Pros</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>
40	      <dt>Cons</dt><dd><ul><li>…</li></ul></dd>
41	      <dt>Cost</dt><dd>…</dd>
42	      <dt>Reversibility</dt><dd>…</dd>
43	    </dl>
44	  </article>
45	  <!-- repeat per alternative -->
46	</div>
47	```
48	
49	The verdict pill (`Chosen` / `Rejected` / em-dash) is optional — apply only if the source explicitly marks one option as chosen.
50	
51	**CSS.**
52	
53	```css
54	.p-alt-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(260px, 1fr)); gap: 16px; margin: 20px 0; }
55	.p-alt-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 20px; transition: border-color 0.15s, transform 0.15s; }
56	.p-alt-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
57	.p-alt-card > header { display: flex; justify-content: space-between; align-items: baseline; gap: 8px; margin-bottom: 12px; }
58	.p-alt-card h3 { margin: 0; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
59	.p-alt-card dl { margin: 0; }
60	.p-alt-card dt { font-size: 12px; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.06em; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 12px; font-weight: 700; }
61	.p-alt-card dd { margin: 4px 0 0; font-size: 15px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }
62	.p-alt-card ul { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 20px; }
63	.p-alt-verdict { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.04em; text-transform: uppercase; padding: 4px 8px; border-radius: 4px; }
64	.p-alt-verdict-chosen { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); }
65	.p-alt-verdict-rejected { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--muted); }
66	```
67	
68	**Accessibility.** Each card is an `<article>` with a unique `<h3>`; cards appear in DOM order so screen readers read them sequentially. The verdict pill text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.
69	
70	---
71	
72	## P2 — Task list with dependencies → vertical timeline
73	
74	**Detect.** A section containing an ordered list (or sequence of H3s named `Task N — title` / `Step N`) where two or more entries mention "depends on", "after", "blocks", or "blocked by" in their first paragraph. Pure task lists without dependency language stay as ordinary ordered lists.
75	
76	**HTML.**
77	
78	```html
79	<ol class="p-timeline">
80	  <li class="p-task" id="task-1">
81	    <div class="p-task-marker" aria-hidden="true">1</div>
82	    <div class="p-task-body">
83	      <h3>Task 1 — name</h3>
84	      <p class="p-task-deps">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>
85	      <p>Task description prose…</p>
86	    </div>
87	  </li>
88	  <!-- repeat per task; dependency lines link to #task-N -->
89	</ol>
90	```
91	
92	**CSS.**
93	
94	```css
95	.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 24px 0; position: relative; }
96	.p-timeline::before { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 17px; top: 6px; bottom: 6px; width: 2px; background: var(--rule); }
97	.p-task { position: relative; padding-left: 52px; margin-bottom: 22px; }
98	.p-task-marker { position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; width: 36px; height: 36px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--surface); border: 2px solid var(--accent); color: var(--accent); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; font-weight: 700; font-size: 14px; font-family: ui-monospace, monospace; }
99	.p-task-body { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; }
100	.p-task-body h3 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
101	.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13.5px; margin: 0 0 8px; }
102	.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }
103	.p-task-deps a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }
104	```
105	
106	**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href="#task-2">Task 2</a>`); marker is `aria-hidden`.
107	
108	---
109	
110	## P3 — Design-doc blocks → tagged H2 + accent panel
111	
112	**Detect.** H2 headings whose text matches one of: `Problem`, `Context`, `Constraints`, `Decision` (or `Recommended approach`), `Consequences`, `Open questions`. Apply per matching H2 — sibling non-matching H2s render with the shell's plain section header.
113	
114	**HTML.** The H2 picks up a small tag pill matching the section type. For `Decision`, the body wraps in a **decision panel** (gradient-filled, indigo border, with a `→ We will` label). Other matches use a thin coloured left-border on the section body but otherwise inherit prose defaults — the type-tag does the visual work.
115	
116	```html
117	<section class="s p-block p-block-decision" id="decision">
118	  <header>
119	    <span class="ix">02</span>
120	    <h2>Decision <span class="p-block-tag p-block-tag-decision">Chosen</span></h2>
121	  </header>
122	  <div class="p-decision-panel">
123	    <span class="p-decision-label">→ We will</span>
124	    Build as a Ruby on Rails 8 monolith using Rails defaults wherever possible.
125	  </div>
126	</section>
127	```
128	
129	```html
130	<section class="s p-block p-block-context" id="context">
131	  <header>
132	    <span class="ix">01</span>
133	    <h2>Context <span class="p-block-tag p-block-tag-context">Background</span></h2>
134	  </header>
135	  <p>…</p>
136	</section>
137	```
138	
139	Tag-label mapping: `Problem` → `Problem` (danger), `Context` → `Background` (muted), `Constraints` → `Limits` (warn), `Decision` → `Chosen` (info / accent), `Consequences` → `Outcomes` (ok), `Open questions` → `Open` (accent-soft).
140	
141	**CSS.**
142	
143	```css
144	.p-block-tag { margin-left: 12px; font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.05em; text-transform: uppercase; vertical-align: 3px; padding: 4px 8px; border-radius: 4px; }
145	.p-block-tag-problem    { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
146	.p-block-tag-context    { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }
147	.p-block-tag-constraints{ background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
148	.p-block-tag-decision   { background: var(--info-soft);   color: var(--info); }
149	.p-block-tag-consequences{ background: var(--ok-soft);    color: var(--ok); }
150	.p-block-tag-open-questions { background: var(--accent-soft); color: var(--accent); }
151	
152	.p-decision-panel {
153	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--info-soft), transparent 80%);
154	  border: 1px solid var(--info); border-radius: 12px;
155	  padding: 22px 24px; margin: 14px 0;
156	  font-size: 18px; line-height: 1.5; color: var(--fg); font-weight: 500;
157	}
158	.p-decision-panel .p-decision-label { display: block; font: 700 13px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.08em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--info); margin-bottom: 10px; }
159	```
160	
161	**Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; the tag text carries the meaning. The decision panel's label (`→ We will`) is a real text node, not a pseudo-element, so screen readers announce it.
162	
163	**Sub-buckets inside `Consequences`.** When a `Consequences` section's body contains H3s (or bold-paragraph headings) named `Positive`, `Negative`, `Neutral` / `Open`, render each as a coloured sub-card rather than as nested H3s:
164	
165	```html
166	<div class="p-conseq-grid">
167	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-positive"><h4>Positive</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
168	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-negative"><h4>Negative</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
169	  <div class="p-conseq p-conseq-neutral"><h4>Neutral</h4><ul>…</ul></div>
170	</div>
171	```
172	
173	```css
174	.p-conseq-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr)); gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
175	.p-conseq { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left-width: 4px; border-radius: 8px; padding: 14px 16px; }
176	.p-conseq h4 { margin: 0 0 10px; font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; }
177	.p-conseq-positive { border-left-color: var(--ok);     } .p-conseq-positive h4 { color: var(--ok); }
178	.p-conseq-negative { border-left-color: var(--danger); } .p-conseq-negative h4 { color: var(--danger); }
179	.p-conseq-neutral  { border-left-color: var(--muted);  } .p-conseq-neutral h4  { color: var(--muted); }
180	```
181	
182	This is a P3 extension, not a separate pattern — the parent section is still `p-block-consequences`.
183	
184	**Consequences as checklist (when flat).** If the `Consequences` body is a single flat bullet list with no sub-buckets, render each bullet as a checklist-card row:
185	
186	```html
187	<div class="p-conseq-list">
188	  <div class="p-conseq-item"><span class="p-conseq-check">→</span><span>All code lives in one repo and deploys as one unit</span></div>
189	</div>
190	```
191	
192	```css
193	.p-conseq-list { display: grid; gap: 10px; margin: 14px 0; }
194	.p-conseq-item { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 32px 1fr; gap: 14px; align-items: start; padding: 14px 16px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.55; color: var(--fg); }
195	.p-conseq-check { width: 24px; height: 24px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: grid; place-items: center; font-size: 14px; font-weight: 700; }
196	```
197	
198	---
199	
200	## P4 — NFRs table → highlighted numeric table
201	
202	**Detect.** Markdown table inside a section whose heading matches `/non-functional|NFRs?/i`, OR any table whose header row contains one of: `Target`, `SLO`, `p95`, `p99`, `Latency`, `Throughput`, `Availability`, `Budget`.
203	
204	**HTML.** Standard `<table>` with `class="p-nfr"`; cells that parse as numbers (optionally with units `ms`, `s`, `%`, `/s`, `KB`, `MB`) get `class="p-num"`.
205	
206	```html
207	<table class="p-nfr">
208	  <thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>Target</th><th>Current</th></tr></thead>
209	  <tbody>
210	    <tr><td>p95 latency</td><td class="p-num">200 ms</td><td class="p-num">340 ms</td></tr>
211	  </tbody>
212	</table>
213	```
214	
215	**CSS.**
216	
217	```css
218	.p-nfr td.p-num { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; font-size: 14px; }
219	.p-nfr tbody tr:hover { background: var(--rule-soft); }
220	```
221	
222	The shell already styles `<table>` cleanly; P4 only adds numeric-cell alignment + hover. **Accessibility.** Standard table semantics; `<thead>`/`<tbody>` preserved.
223	
224	---
225	
226	## P5 — Open questions → checklist with owner / due
227	
228	**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?/i`. Body is a bullet list; each bullet may end with parenthetical metadata `(owner: name, due: 2026-06-01)` or markdown task-list syntax `- [ ]` / `- [x]`.
229	
230	**HTML.**
231	
232	```html
233	<ul class="p-questions">
234	  <li class="p-q">
235	    <input type="checkbox" disabled>
236	    <span class="p-q-text">Should we use Postgres or DynamoDB for the audit log?</span>
237	    <span class="p-q-meta"><span class="pill pill-meta">@anton</span><span class="pill pill-meta">due 2026-06-01</span></span>
238	  </li>
239	</ul>
240	```
241	
242	**CSS.**
243	
244	```css
245	.p-questions { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 20px 0; }
246	.p-q { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto; gap: 14px; align-items: center; padding: 12px 16px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; margin-bottom: 8px; }
247	.p-q input[type=checkbox] { accent-color: var(--accent); transform: scale(1.1); }
248	.p-q-text { color: var(--fg); font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.5; }
249	.p-q-meta { display: flex; gap: 6px; }
250	```
251	
252	Owner / due metadata reuses the shell's `.pill .pill-meta` utility. **Accessibility.** Checkbox is `disabled` (read-only display); metadata is visible text, not buried in `title=`.
253	
254	---
255	
256	## P6 — Diff / before-after → two-column annotated diff
257	
258	**Detect.** Two adjacent fenced code blocks within the same H3 / paragraph where the first is labelled (in info string or preceding line) `before`, `was`, `old`, `current`, and the second `after`, `now`, `new`, `proposed`. Also: any code block with info string `diff` renders inline with `+`/`-` line colouring.
259	
260	**HTML (two-column).**
261	
262	```html
263	<div class="p-diff">
264	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-before">
265	    <header>Before</header>
266	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
267	  </div>
268	  <div class="p-diff-side p-diff-after">
269	    <header>After</header>
270	    <pre><code>…</code></pre>
271	  </div>
272	</div>
273	```
274	
275	**HTML (inline diff).** Standard `<pre><code class="language-diff">` with each line wrapped: `<span class="p-diff-add">+ …</span>`, `<span class="p-diff-del">- …</span>`.
276	
277	**CSS.**
278	
279	```css
280	.p-diff { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
281	.p-diff-side header { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }
282	.p-diff-before pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--danger); }
283	.p-diff-after  pre { border-left: 3px solid var(--ok); }
284	.p-diff-add { background: var(--ok-soft); color: var(--ok); display: block; }
285	.p-diff-del { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); display: block; }
286	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-diff { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
287	```
288	
289	**Accessibility.** Headers (`Before` / `After`) carry the meaning; colour stripe is supplemental.
290	
291	---
292	
293	## P7 — Roadmap Now / Next / Later → three-column kanban
294	
295	**Detect.** Three sibling H2s or H3s with text exactly matching (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant): `Now`, `Next`, `Later`, **AND** each of those sections' body is predominantly a single `<ul>`/`<ol>` (no nested H3s, no Problem/Appetite/ICE callout sub-structure). If only two of the three columns are present and the bullet-only condition holds, still render as a kanban with the missing column empty. If the bullet-only condition fails (sections contain rich prose, callouts, or H3 sub-items per column), **do not apply** — fall back to prose so the column content stays readable.
296	
297	**HTML.**
298	
299	```html
300	<div class="p-kanban">
301	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="now">
302	    <header><span class="p-kanban-dot"></span>Now</header>
303	    <ul>
304	      <li>Item one</li>
305	      <li>Item two</li>
306	    </ul>
307	  </div>
308	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="next"> … </div>
309	  <div class="p-kanban-col" data-col="later"> … </div>
310	</div>
311	```
312	
313	**CSS.**
314	
315	```css
316	.p-kanban { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(3, 1fr); gap: 14px; margin: 24px 0; }
317	.p-kanban-col { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; min-height: 160px; }
318	.p-kanban-col header { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; font: 700 13px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; padding-bottom: 12px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); margin-bottom: 12px; }
319	.p-kanban-dot { width: 9px; height: 9px; border-radius: 50%; }
320	.p-kanban-col[data-col="now"]   header { color: var(--ok); }     .p-kanban-col[data-col="now"]   .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--ok); }
321	.p-kanban-col[data-col="next"]  header { color: var(--info); }   .p-kanban-col[data-col="next"]  .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--info); }
322	.p-kanban-col[data-col="later"] header { color: var(--muted); }  .p-kanban-col[data-col="later"] .p-kanban-dot { background: var(--muted); }
323	.p-kanban-col ul { padding-left: 20px; margin: 0; font-size: 14.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }
324	.p-kanban-col li { margin: 8px 0; }
325	@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-kanban { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }
326	```
327	
328	**Accessibility.** Each column is keyboard-navigable as ordinary text; the dot + header colour are supplemental to the label.
329	
330	---
331	
332	## P8 — ICE scores → coloured score chips
333	
334	**Detect.** Markdown table whose header row contains EITHER (a) `Impact`, `Confidence`, and `Ease` (in any order, plus an optional `Score` column), OR (b) a single `ICE` column alongside at least one of `Confidence` / `Slot` / `Theme` / `Kano`. Numeric cells are graded on the same red/amber/green band regardless of column. Em-dash placeholders (`—`) render as a muted chip.
335	
336	**HTML.**
337	
338	```html
339	<table class="p-ice">
340	  <thead><tr><th>Idea</th><th>Impact</th><th>Confidence</th><th>Ease</th><th>ICE</th></tr></thead>
341	  <tbody>
342	    <tr>
343	      <td>Onboarding revamp</td>
344	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="green">9</span></td>
345	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="amber">5</span></td>
346	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="red">3</span></td>
347	      <td><span class="p-chip" data-band="amber">135</span></td>
348	    </tr>
349	  </tbody>
350	</table>
351	```
352	
353	**CSS.**
354	
355	```css
356	.p-ice .p-chip { display: inline-block; min-width: 32px; padding: 4px 10px; border-radius: 999px; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 13px; text-align: center; font-weight: 700; }
357	.p-chip[data-band="red"]   { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }
358	.p-chip[data-band="amber"] { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }
359	.p-chip[data-band="green"] { background: var(--ok-soft);     color: var(--ok); }
360	.p-chip[data-band="muted"] { background: var(--rule-soft);   color: var(--muted); }
361	.p-ice td:not(:first-child) { text-align: center; }
362	```
363	
364	Chip bands: `1–3 → red`, `4–6 → amber`, `7–10 → green`, em-dash / missing → `muted`. ICE totals (any value above 10) are graded against the same 50 / 100 / 200 thresholds used for sequencing in roadmaps that follow `PRODUCT_RULES`. **Accessibility.** Colour is supplemental; numeric value is the source of truth.
365	
366	---
367	
368	## P9 — YAML frontmatter → metadata grid (lifted into doc head)
369	
370	**Detect.** Source markdown begins with a YAML frontmatter block delimited by `---` on its own line, before the H1. Common in ADRs and design docs (fields like `id`, `status`, `date`, `authors`, `linear`, `supersedes`).
371	
372	**HTML.** Frontmatter fields **lift into the shell's `{{meta-grid}}` slot** under the doc head — they do not render as a separate block in the body. The shell's `.meta-grid` styles already cover the visual treatment; P9 only specifies the *lifting* behaviour. The `status` field also lifts into `{{header-pills}}` via the P10 pill treatment.
373	
374	```html
375	<!-- inside the shell's <dl class="meta-grid"> -->
376	<dt>id</dt>      <dd>ADR-engagement-modifier-constants</dd>
377	<dt>authors</dt> <dd>Anton Babushkin</dd>
378	<dt>linear</dt>  <dd><a href="https://linear.app/…">ABA-110</a></dd>
379	<dt>supersedes</dt><dd>—</dd>
380	<dt>source</dt>  <dd>docs/adrs/engagement-modifier-constants.md</dd>
381	<dt>rendered</dt><dd>2026-05-17</dd>
382	```
383	
384	`source` and `rendered` are always included; frontmatter fields fold in alphabetically after them, except `status` (which becomes a header pill, not a grid row) and `date` (which becomes a date pill in the header). **Do not drop frontmatter on the floor** — readers use it to anchor the doc.
385	
386	**Accessibility.** Definition-list semantics; screen readers announce each key-value pair.
387	
388	---
389	
390	## P10 — Status / state values → coloured pill
391	
392	**Detect.** A frontmatter `status:` field, OR a top-of-doc bold line matching `/\*\*Status:\*\*\s+(\w+)/i`, OR a single-cell `Status` column in a table. Recognised values (case-insensitive): `accepted`, `proposed`, `draft`, `rejected`, `superseded`, `deprecated`, `flagged`, `ok`.
393	
394	**HTML.** When detected from frontmatter or top-of-doc bold line, the pill **lifts into the shell's `{{header-pills}}` slot** (using the shell's `.pill` utility — no new CSS needed):
395	
396	```html
397	<span class="pill pill-accepted"><span class="dot"></span>Accepted</span>
398	```
399	
400	When detected inside a table cell, render in-place:
401	
402	```html
403	<td><span class="pill pill-accepted"><span class="dot"></span>Accepted</span></td>
404	```
405	
406	The shell already defines `.pill-accepted`, `.pill-proposed`, `.pill-rejected`, `.pill-superseded`, etc. P10 adds no new CSS — it specifies the *detection + placement* contract. **Accessibility.** Label text carries the meaning; colour is supplemental.
407	
408	---
409	
410	## P11 — Rationale "Why X" blocks → hover-state cards
411	
412	**Detect.** A section heading matches `/rationale/i` AND its body contains two-or-more bold-paragraph headers (`**Why X:**` / `**Why not Y:**`) each followed by a bullet list. Common in ADRs that consider multiple alternatives without dedicated H3s per option.
413	
414	**HTML.** Each bold paragraph becomes a card heading; the following list is the card body. Cards stack in a single-column grid (one per row) so each option gets full line-length for its bullets, but pick up an indigo border on hover and a directional chevron in the title — `✓` for `Why X`, `✗` for `Why not Y`.
415	
416	```html
417	<div class="p-why-grid">
418	  <div class="p-why-card p-why-pro">
419	    <h3><span class="p-why-chev">✓</span>Why Rails</h3>
420	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
421	  </div>
422	  <div class="p-why-card p-why-con">
423	    <h3><span class="p-why-chev">✗</span>Why not a separate frontend</h3>
424	    <ul><li>…</li></ul>
425	  </div>
426	</div>
427	```
428	
429	**CSS.**
430	
431	```css
432	.p-why-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; }
433	.p-why-card { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 22px; transition: border-color 0.15s; }
434	.p-why-card:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
435	.p-why-card h3 { margin: 0 0 14px; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; color: var(--fg); }
436	.p-why-chev { font-size: 18px; line-height: 1; font-weight: 700; }
437	.p-why-pro .p-why-chev { color: var(--ok); }
438	.p-why-con .p-why-chev { color: var(--warn); }
439	.p-why-card ul { margin: 0; padding: 0; list-style: none; }
440	.p-why-card li { position: relative; padding: 6px 0 6px 20px; font-size: 15px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.6; }
441	.p-why-card li::before { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 4px; top: 15px; width: 5px; height: 5px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--muted); }
442	```
443	
444	**Precedence.** P11 ranks below P1 (Alternatives). When both could match (`Rationale` section whose options also have `Pros:`/`Cons:`), P1's side-by-side card grid is the stronger treatment.
445	
446	**Accessibility.** `<h3>` keeps headings in the document outline; lists keep list semantics; chevron is a real text node (not pseudo-content) so screen readers announce it.
447	
448	---
449	
450	## P12 — Labelled rubric cards (C1, C2, FR3 …) → repeated sub-field cards
451	
452	**Detect.** Two or more sibling H3s whose text matches `/^([A-Z]+\d+)\s+[—–-]\s+/` (e.g. `C1 — Deadband: ±2%`, `FR3 — Idempotent writes`, `OQ5 — …`), AND each section repeats the same set of bold-paragraph sub-fields (e.g. **Reasoning**, **Alternative considered**, **Revision trigger**). The identifier prefix (`C`, `FR`, `OQ`, `NFR`, `ADR`) is rendered as a pill on the card header.
453	
454	**HTML.**
455	
456	```html
457	<div class="p-rubric" id="c1">
458	  <h3><span class="p-rubric-id">C1</span> Deadband: ±2% YoY</h3>
459	  <div class="p-rubric-formula">If <code>abs(yoy_change) &lt; 0.02</code>, set <code>direction = 0</code> …</div>
460	  <h4>Reasoning</h4><p>…</p>
461	  <h4>Alternative considered</h4><p>…</p>
462	  <h4>Revision trigger</h4><p>…</p>
463	</div>
464	```
465	
466	The first paragraph (often a one-line formal definition or formula) becomes `p-rubric-formula`. Subsequent bold-paragraph sub-fields become `<h4>` headings inside the card.
467	
468	**CSS.**
469	
470	```css
471	.p-rubric { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 10px; padding: 18px 22px; margin: 20px 0; transition: border-color 0.15s; }
472	.p-rubric:hover { border-color: var(--accent); }
473	.p-rubric h3 { margin: 0 0 10px; display: flex; gap: 12px; align-items: baseline; font-size: 17px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }
474	.p-rubric-id { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 13px; padding: 4px 9px; background: var(--accent); color: white; border-radius: 4px; letter-spacing: 0.02em; font-weight: 700; }
475	.p-rubric h4 { font: 700 12px/1 "Inter", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin: 16px 0 6px; }
476	.p-rubric-formula { background: var(--accent-soft); border-left: 3px solid var(--accent); padding: 12px 16px; margin: 12px 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.55; border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; color: var(--fg); }
477	.p-rubric p { margin: 0 0 10px; font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.6; color: var(--fg-soft); }
478	```
479	
480	**Why a dedicated pattern.** Plain-prose rendering of a 6-card ADR section (C1–C6 with Reasoning / Alternative / Revision trigger each) produces a wall of repeating H3+H4 that the reader has to mentally segment. The card boundary plus the identifier pill makes "which constant am I reading" answerable at a glance.
481	
482	**Accessibility.** Heading levels (`<h3>` outer, `<h4>` inner) preserve the document outline; the card boundary is decorative.
483	
484	---
485	
486	## Fallback
487	
488	A section that matches no pattern renders with the shell's section-header treatment (`<section class="s"><header><span class="ix">NN</span><h2>…</h2></header>`) and the shell's prose defaults (`<p>`, `<ul>`, `<table>`, `<pre>`, `<blockquote>`). The fallback is "well-typeset doc," not raw HTML — false-positive pattern matches degrade review trust faster than missing treatments.
489	
490	## Adding a new pattern
491	
492	When a new pattern earns its slot:
493	
494	1. Add a section here following the same six-part structure: heading, **Detect**, **HTML**, **CSS**, **Accessibility**, brief rationale.
495	2. Number it sequentially (`P13 — …`) so cross-references stay stable.
496	3. Confirm the CSS uses shell colour tokens (`--accent`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--info`, `--danger`, `-soft` variants) — no ad-hoc hex values.
497	4. Confirm the CSS only adds new selectors prefixed `p-` — it must not override any rule in `html-skeleton.md`.
498	5. If the detection signal could overlap an existing pattern, document the precedence rule explicitly. The first-match-wins ordering of this file is the resolution mechanism.
499	
```

  "old_string": "---\n\n## Fallback",
  "new_string": "---\n\n## P13 — Summary strip → top-of-doc KPI cells\n\n**Detect.** The first content block after the doc header is a short bullet list or definition list of 3–6 key:value pairs where keys are short labels (`Effort`, `Surfaces touched`, `New tables`, `Feature flag`, `Owner`, `Decision deadline`, `Cost`, `Status`) and values are short atomic phrases (a duration, a count, a flag name, a date, a dollar figure). Also matches when the source uses a YAML-frontmatter-style block under a `## Summary` or `## At a glance` heading. **Does not apply** when the same shape appears mid-doc — only the first such block lifts into the summary strip; subsequent ones render as ordinary `<dl>`s.\n\n**HTML.** Lifts into a 4-up grid directly under the doc head (above the first `<section>`):\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-summary\">\n  <div class=\"p-summary-cell\"><div class=\"p-summary-k\">Effort</div><div class=\"p-summary-v p-summary-v-accent\">~2 weeks</div></div>\n  <div class=\"p-summary-cell\"><div class=\"p-summary-k\">Surfaces touched</div><div class=\"p-summary-v\">3 packages</div></div>\n  <div class=\"p-summary-cell\"><div class=\"p-summary-k\">New tables</div><div class=\"p-summary-v\">2</div></div>\n  <div class=\"p-summary-cell\"><div class=\"p-summary-k\">Feature flag</div><div class=\"p-summary-v\">task_comments_v1</div></div>\n</div>\n```\n\nThe first cell's value gets `p-summary-v-accent` when it carries the headline number (effort, cost, deadline) — at most one per strip. Strip auto-collapses to 2-up at narrow widths.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-summary { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 1fr); gap: 14px; margin: 0 0 32px; }\n.p-summary-cell { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; }\n.p-summary-k { font: 700 11px/1 \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }\n.p-summary-v { font-size: 17px; font-weight: 600; color: var(--fg); line-height: 1.3; }\n.p-summary-v-accent { color: var(--accent); }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-summary { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr); } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Each cell is plain text; label and value are independent text nodes. **Why a dedicated pattern.** A plan or design doc that opens with \"this will take two weeks, touch three packages, and ship behind `task_comments_v1`\" gives every subsequent reader an anchor for \"is this proportionate?\" before they hit the first detail.\n\n---\n\n## P14 — Data flow / architecture → inline SVG diagram\n\n**Detect.** A section heading matches `/data flow|architecture|sequence|system( context)?|fan[- ]?out|pipeline/i` AND its body contains EITHER (a) a fenced code block tagged ` ```svg ` containing literal `<svg>` markup, OR (b) a markdown description of nodes-and-edges that the agent has already converted to SVG during conversion. The skill **does not** auto-generate SVG from prose — agents author the SVG explicitly when they identify a diagram-worthy concept.\n\n**HTML.** The SVG is wrapped in a framed panel with a caption beneath. Solid lines and dashed lines carry semantic meaning by convention; the caption explains the convention used.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-diagram\">\n  <svg viewBox=\"0 0 860 340\" xmlns=\"http://www.w3.org/2000/svg\" role=\"img\" aria-label=\"Comment write path and realtime fan-out\">\n    <defs>\n      <marker id=\"arr\" viewBox=\"0 0 10 10\" refX=\"9\" refY=\"5\" markerWidth=\"7\" markerHeight=\"7\" orient=\"auto-start-reverse\">\n        <path d=\"M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z\" fill=\"currentColor\"/>\n      </marker>\n      <marker id=\"arr-accent\" viewBox=\"0 0 10 10\" refX=\"9\" refY=\"5\" markerWidth=\"7\" markerHeight=\"7\" orient=\"auto-start-reverse\">\n        <path d=\"M0,0 L10,5 L0,10z\" fill=\"var(--accent)\"/>\n      </marker>\n    </defs>\n    <!-- Node template -->\n    <g class=\"p-node\">\n      <rect x=\"20\" y=\"20\" width=\"180\" height=\"54\" rx=\"10\"/>\n      <text x=\"110\" y=\"43\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-title\">&lt;Composer&gt;</text>\n      <text x=\"110\" y=\"60\" text-anchor=\"middle\" class=\"p-node-sub\">apps/web</text>\n    </g>\n    <!-- Edges: stroke=\"currentColor\" = solid request path; class=\"p-edge-async\" = dashed realtime path -->\n    <path d=\"M110 74 L110 150\" marker-end=\"url(#arr)\" class=\"p-edge\"/>\n    <path d=\"M520 177 L660 177\" marker-end=\"url(#arr-accent)\" class=\"p-edge-async\"/>\n    <!-- Edge label -->\n    <text x=\"240\" y=\"170\" class=\"p-edge-label\">mutate</text>\n  </svg>\n  <p class=\"p-diagram-caption\">Solid = request/response. Dashed = realtime fan-out. The composer never waits on the dashed path.</p>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-diagram { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; padding: 24px; margin: 20px 0; overflow-x: auto; }\n.p-diagram svg { display: block; min-width: 100%; max-width: 100%; height: auto; color: var(--muted); }\n.p-diagram .p-node rect { fill: var(--bg); stroke: var(--rule); stroke-width: 1.5; }\n.p-diagram .p-node-title { font: 600 12px ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; fill: var(--fg); }\n.p-diagram .p-node-sub { font: 10.5px ui-monospace, monospace; fill: var(--muted); }\n.p-diagram .p-edge { stroke: currentColor; stroke-width: 1.5; fill: none; }\n.p-diagram .p-edge-async { stroke: var(--accent); stroke-width: 1.5; fill: none; stroke-dasharray: 5 4; }\n.p-diagram .p-edge-label { font: 10.5px ui-monospace, monospace; fill: var(--muted); }\n.p-diagram-caption { font-size: 13.5px; color: var(--muted); margin: 12px 0 0; line-height: 1.55; }\n```\n\n**Authoring guidance for the agent.** Use the four node-classes (`p-node` for default, `p-node p-node-emphasis` for the persistence layer or any single \"focus\" box — fills with `var(--fg)` background and `var(--bg)` text). Limit to ~7 nodes and ~10 edges; if the diagram needs more, split it. Edge labels must be ≤ 24 chars; longer explanations go in the caption.\n\n**Accessibility.** `<svg role=\"img\" aria-label=\"…\">` provides a short description; the caption beneath provides the long description. Text inside the SVG is selectable. **No remote fonts** — the SVG inherits the page font stack.\n\n---\n\n## P15 — Mockup frame → labelled UI sketch\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/mockup|wireframe|sketch|prototype/i` AND body contains 1–3 sub-blocks separated by H3s or bold paragraph headers that look like artefact labels (`A · Thread inside a task card`, `Variant 1`, `Empty state`). Mockup contents can be either (a) inline HTML the agent has authored to approximate the UI, or (b) an inline SVG sketch.\n\n**HTML.** Each mockup is a framed card with a small label bar and a body area. Two-up grid by default; collapses to single column at narrow widths.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-mocks\">\n  <figure class=\"p-mock\">\n    <figcaption class=\"p-mock-label\">A · Thread inside a task card</figcaption>\n    <div class=\"p-mock-body\">\n      <!-- agent-authored UI sketch goes here -->\n    </div>\n  </figure>\n  <figure class=\"p-mock\">\n    <figcaption class=\"p-mock-label\">B · Sidebar unread digest</figcaption>\n    <div class=\"p-mock-body\"> … </div>\n  </figure>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-mocks { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(320px, 1fr)); gap: 20px; margin: 20px 0; }\n.p-mock { margin: 0; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; overflow: hidden; }\n.p-mock-label { padding: 10px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--rule-soft); font: 700 12px/1 \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.06em; text-transform: uppercase; color: var(--muted); }\n.p-mock-body { padding: 20px; background: var(--bg); }\n```\n\n**Authoring guidance.** Mockups are deliberately sketchy — they exist to align reviewers on layout, not to look pixel-final. Real UI primitives the agent can lean on: rounded `<div>`s for cards, small circular `<div>`s for avatars (initials inside), dashed-border divs for \"image goes here,\" `<input disabled>` for form fields. The aim is \"we agree on the shape,\" not \"this is the final design.\"\n\n**Accessibility.** `<figure>` + `<figcaption>` keeps the label tied to the mockup for screen readers. Mockup bodies should be ARIA-`role=\"presentation\"` if they contain non-functional interactive-looking elements (a `<button>` that doesn't do anything is worse than a styled `<div>`).\n\n---\n\n## P16 — Code panel → file-labelled dark block\n\n**Detect.** Any fenced code block that has BOTH (a) an info string naming the language AND (b) a preceding line or paragraph that names a file path (`apps/web/hooks/useAddComment.ts`, `packages/db/migrations/0042_comments.sql`). The file path is detected as a relative-path-looking token containing at least one `/` and ending in a recognised extension. Plain fenced blocks without a file label fall through to the shell's default `<pre>` styling. The P6 two-column diff treatment takes precedence when both apply.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<figure class=\"p-code\">\n  <figcaption class=\"p-code-file\">apps/web/hooks/useAddComment.ts</figcaption>\n  <pre class=\"p-code-body\"><code class=\"language-ts\">…</code></pre>\n</figure>\n```\n\nWhen two or more code panels appear in the same section back-to-back, they render in a 2-up grid so reviewers can scan them in parallel (e.g. migration + hook):\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-code-grid\">\n  <figure class=\"p-code\"> … </figure>\n  <figure class=\"p-code\"> … </figure>\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-code { margin: 16px 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; }\n.p-code-file { font: 12px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); margin-bottom: 8px; }\n.p-code-body { background: var(--fg); color: #e8e6de; border-radius: 12px; padding: 18px 20px; overflow-x: auto; margin: 0; }\n.p-code-body code { font: 12.5px/1.65 ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: inherit; background: transparent; padding: 0; }\n.p-code-body .tok-kw  { color: var(--accent); }\n.p-code-body .tok-str { color: var(--ok); }\n.p-code-body .tok-cm  { color: var(--muted); font-style: italic; }\n.p-code-body .tok-fn  { color: #c9b98a; }\n.p-code-grid { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr; gap: 20px; margin: 16px 0; }\n@media (max-width: 900px) { .p-code-grid { grid-template-columns: 1fr; } }\n```\n\n**Token wrapping.** The agent applies four span classes inline (`tok-kw` for keywords, `tok-str` for string literals, `tok-cm` for comments, `tok-fn` for function/identifier names). Don't attempt full lexer-grade highlighting — the four tokens are enough to give the panel hierarchy without misleading the reader. If the agent isn't confident about a token, leave it un-spanned (the default body colour reads cleanly).\n\n**Accessibility.** `<figure>` + `<figcaption>` ties the file path to the code. Dark background + light text passes AA at 14.5:1 (`#e8e6de` on `var(--fg)` `#0a0a0f`).\n\n---\n\n## P17 — Risks & mitigations → severity-tagged table\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/risks?( ?(and|&) ?mitigations?)?|threats?|failure modes?/i` AND body is a markdown table with at least these columns (case-insensitive, partial match OK): `Risk` (or `Description`), `Severity` (or `Sev` / `Likelihood`), `Mitigation` (or `Response`). An optional `Owner` column is preserved. Severity values are matched against `high|med(ium)?|low|critical` (case-insensitive).\n\n**HTML.** Three- or four-column grid (not a real `<table>`, because we want the severity cell to be a chip in a narrow centred column). Borders form the visual table.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-risks\">\n  <div class=\"p-risks-row p-risks-head\">\n    <div>Risk</div><div>Sev</div><div>Mitigation</div>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-risks-row\">\n    <div>Realtime duplicate: socket append races with HTTP response.</div>\n    <div><span class=\"p-sev p-sev-high\">HIGH</span></div>\n    <div>Dedupe on server-assigned <code>id</code>; temp rows filtered on reconcile.</div>\n  </div>\n  <!-- repeat rows -->\n</div>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-risks { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; overflow: hidden; background: var(--surface); margin: 20px 0; }\n.p-risks-row { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1.6fr 80px 1.6fr; }\n.p-risks-row + .p-risks-row { border-top: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n.p-risks-row > div { padding: 14px 18px; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.55; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n.p-risks-row > div + div { border-left: 1px solid var(--rule); }\n.p-risks-head > div { background: var(--rule-soft); color: var(--fg); font: 700 12px/1 \"Inter\", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif; letter-spacing: 0.05em; text-transform: uppercase; padding: 12px 18px; }\n.p-sev { display: inline-block; font: 700 11px/1 ui-monospace, monospace; padding: 4px 9px; border-radius: 6px; letter-spacing: 0.04em; }\n.p-sev-high     { background: var(--danger-soft); color: var(--danger); }\n.p-sev-critical { background: var(--danger);      color: white; }\n.p-sev-med      { background: var(--warn-soft);   color: var(--warn); }\n.p-sev-low      { background: var(--ok-soft);     color: var(--ok); }\n@media (max-width: 720px) {\n  .p-risks-row { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }\n  .p-risks-row > div + div { border-left: none; border-top: 1px dashed var(--rule); }\n  .p-risks-head { display: none; }\n}\n```\n\n**Optional owner column.** When the source table has an Owner column, switch the grid to `1.5fr 80px 1.5fr 120px` and add a fourth `<div>` per row. Owner renders as plain text or a `pill pill-meta` if it looks like a handle (`@anton`).\n\n**Accessibility.** Severity label text (`HIGH` / `MED` / `LOW`) carries the meaning; colour is supplemental. The grid uses `<div>`s rather than `<table>` because the severity column is intentionally narrow and centre-aligned — a real table here forces awkward column-width compromises. Screen readers still announce each row's content in order; for users who require table semantics, the source markdown table is the canonical form.\n\n---\n\n## Fallback"
}
```

  "old_string": "**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ol class=\"p-timeline\">\n  <li class=\"p-task\" id=\"task-1\">\n    <div class=\"p-task-marker\" aria-hidden=\"true\">1</div>\n    <div class=\"p-task-body\">\n      <h3>Task 1 — name</h3>\n      <p class=\"p-task-deps\">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>\n      <p>Task description prose…</p>\n    </div>\n  </li>\n  <!-- repeat per task; dependency lines link to #task-N -->\n</ol>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 24px 0; position: relative; }\n.p-timeline::before { content: \"\"; position: absolute; left: 17px; top: 6px; bottom: 6px; width: 2px; background: var(--rule); }\n.p-task { position: relative; padding-left: 52px; margin-bottom: 22px; }\n.p-task-marker { position: absolute; left: 0; top: 0; width: 36px; height: 36px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--surface); border: 2px solid var(--accent); color: var(--accent); display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; font-weight: 700; font-size: 14px; font-family: ui-monospace, monospace; }\n.p-task-body { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 18px; }\n.p-task-body h3 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 16px; font-weight: 700; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13.5px; margin: 0 0 8px; }\n.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }\n.p-task-deps a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href=\"#task-2\">Task 2</a>`); marker is `aria-hidden`.",
  "new_string": "**HTML.** Three-column row per task: optional `when` (date/sprint label), dot-and-line rail, body. The rail column is always present so the timeline reads as a connected sequence; the `when` column is omitted across the whole timeline if no task has scheduling info.\n\n```html\n<ol class=\"p-timeline\">\n  <li class=\"p-task\" id=\"task-1\">\n    <div class=\"p-task-when\">Week 1 · Mon–Tue</div>\n    <div class=\"p-task-rail\" aria-hidden=\"true\">\n      <span class=\"p-task-dot p-task-dot-done\"></span>\n      <span class=\"p-task-line\"></span>\n    </div>\n    <div class=\"p-task-body\">\n      <h3>Schema &amp; API contract</h3>\n      <p>New <code>comments</code> and <code>comment_reads</code> tables, migrations, tRPC router stubs. Contract reviewed before anything else lands.</p>\n      <p class=\"p-task-deps\">Depends on: <em>none</em></p>\n      <div class=\"p-task-tags\">\n        <span class=\"p-task-tag\">packages/db</span>\n        <span class=\"p-task-tag\">packages/api</span>\n        <span class=\"p-task-tag\">migration 0042</span>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  </li>\n  <!-- repeat per task; final <li> drops the trailing line -->\n</ol>\n```\n\nThe dot has two states: default (`p-task-dot`, hollow ring in `--accent`) and done (`p-task-dot-done`, solid `--ok`). The line is omitted on the final task. Tag chips are optional — emit them when the source mentions concrete packages, components, file paths, or migration IDs.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-timeline { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 24px 0; }\n.p-task { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 132px 28px 1fr; gap: 0 18px; }\n.p-task[data-no-when] { grid-template-columns: 28px 1fr; }\n.p-task-when { text-align: right; font: 12.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); padding-top: 6px; }\n.p-task-rail { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; }\n.p-task-dot { width: 14px; height: 14px; border-radius: 50%; background: var(--bg); border: 3px solid var(--accent); margin-top: 6px; flex-shrink: 0; }\n.p-task-dot-done { background: var(--ok); border-color: var(--ok); }\n.p-task-line { width: 2px; flex: 1; background: var(--rule); margin: 4px 0; }\n.p-task:last-child .p-task-line { display: none; }\n.p-task-body { padding-bottom: 28px; }\n.p-task-body h3 { margin: 0 0 6px; font-size: 17px; font-weight: 600; color: var(--fg); }\n.p-task-body p { font-size: 14.5px; color: var(--fg-soft); margin: 0 0 10px; line-height: 1.55; max-width: 65ch; }\n.p-task-deps { color: var(--muted); font-size: 13.5px; }\n.p-task-deps a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }\n.p-task-deps a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }\n.p-task-tags { display: flex; gap: 8px; flex-wrap: wrap; margin-top: 8px; }\n.p-task-tag { font: 11.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; background: var(--rule-soft); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 6px; padding: 3px 8px; color: var(--fg-soft); }\n@media (max-width: 720px) { .p-task, .p-task[data-no-when] { grid-template-columns: 22px 1fr; } .p-task-when { display: none; } }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Still an `<ol>` — screen readers announce the task count and order. Dependency phrases become inline links (`<a href=\"#task-2\">Task 2</a>`); rail (`p-task-rail`) is `aria-hidden`. The `when` label is plain text and reads as part of each task."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/pde-skills/references/html-patterns.md",
  "old_string": "## P5 — Open questions → checklist with owner / due\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?/i`. Body is a bullet list; each bullet may end with parenthetical metadata `(owner: name, due: 2026-06-01)` or markdown task-list syntax `- [ ]` / `- [x]`.\n\n**HTML.**\n\n```html\n<ul class=\"p-questions\">\n  <li class=\"p-q\">\n    <input type=\"checkbox\" disabled>\n    <span class=\"p-q-text\">Should we use Postgres or DynamoDB for the audit log?</span>\n    <span class=\"p-q-meta\"><span class=\"pill pill-meta\">@anton</span><span class=\"pill pill-meta\">due 2026-06-01</span></span>\n  </li>\n</ul>\n```\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-questions { list-style: none; padding: 0; margin: 20px 0; }\n.p-q { display: grid; grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto; gap: 14px; align-items: center; padding: 12px 16px; background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 8px; margin-bottom: 8px; }\n.p-q input[type=checkbox] { accent-color: var(--accent); transform: scale(1.1); }\n.p-q-text { color: var(--fg); font-size: 15px; line-height: 1.5; }\n.p-q-meta { display: flex; gap: 6px; }\n```\n\nOwner / due metadata reuses the shell's `.pill .pill-meta` utility. **Accessibility.** Checkbox is `disabled` (read-only display); metadata is visible text, not buried in `title=`.",
  "new_string": "## P5 — Open questions → left-accent decision cards\n\n**Detect.** Section heading matches `/open questions?|outstanding questions?|to decide|decisions? to make/i`. Body is either a bullet list (one item per question) or a sequence of bold-paragraph headers (`**Do we allow editing?**`) each followed by a short paragraph of context. Optional trailing metadata `(decide with: design, before slice 2)` or `(owner: @anton, due: 2026-06-01)` lifts into a footer line.\n\n**HTML.** Each question becomes a vertically-stacked card with a clay accent on the left, a bold question, a context paragraph, and an optional owner/deadline footer in mono.\n\n```html\n<div class=\"p-questions\">\n  <div class=\"p-q\">\n    <div class=\"p-q-title\">Do we allow editing, or only delete-and-repost?</div>\n    <div class=\"p-q-body\">Editing needs an <code>edited_at</code> column and an \"edited\" affordance. Delete-and-repost is simpler but loses the reply anchor. Leaning toward delete-only for v1.</div>\n    <div class=\"p-q-owner\">Decide with · design, before slice 2</div>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"p-q\">\n    <div class=\"p-q-title\">Email digest cadence when a user has the app closed</div>\n    <div class=\"p-q-body\">Immediate-per-mention will be noisy. Proposal: batch on a 15-minute window, collapse to one email per task, and respect quiet hours from the existing settings table.</div>\n    <div class=\"p-q-owner\">Decide with · platform, before slice 4</div>\n  </div>\n</div>\n```\n\nThe `p-q-owner` footer is optional. When metadata isn't present in the source, omit the `<div>` entirely rather than render an empty footer.\n\n**CSS.**\n\n```css\n.p-questions { display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 14px; margin: 20px 0; max-width: 820px; }\n.p-q { background: var(--surface); border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-left: 4px solid var(--accent); border-radius: 10px; padding: 16px 20px; }\n.p-q-title { font-weight: 600; font-size: 15.5px; color: var(--fg); margin-bottom: 6px; }\n.p-q-body { font-size: 14px; color: var(--fg-soft); line-height: 1.55; }\n.p-q-body code { font-size: 0.92em; padding: 1px 5px; background: var(--rule-soft); border-radius: 3px; }\n.p-q-owner { font: 11.5px ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, monospace; color: var(--muted); margin-top: 10px; }\n```\n\n**Accessibility.** Plain text hierarchy (title → body → owner). The accent border is decorative — the bold title carries the visual weight and reads cleanly on screen readers."
}
```

1	# Implementation Plan: Portable Nestl
2	
3	Spec: [SPEC.md](./../../SPEC.md).
4	
5	## Overview
6	
7	Make Nestl runnable on any macOS machine Anton owns. De-hardcode paths, extract personal profile into gitignored YAML, declare Homebrew deps, template the launchd plist, and document the full fresh-Mac flow.
8	
9	## Architecture decisions
10	
11	- **Profile via YAML, not env vars** — complex nested data (weights, pet prefs) fits YAML better; matches Rails config conventions; trivial to diff/version-control the example file.
12	- **Plist as ERB template, not sed** — Ruby ERB is already a project dep; sed/bash variable expansion in plists is fragile.
13	- **Ship export immediately (done), import later** — export was the backup-safety bottleneck; import only matters when a second machine exists.
14	- **Keep \****`master.key`**\*\* manual paste, no automation** — per-machine paste from 1Password is fine for single-user; any automation adds attack surface for zero benefit.
15	- **No shell test framework** — manual fresh-Mac checklist documented in `docs/fresh-mac-verification.md` and run rarely.
16
